### [AMD-24] The card width was the last link in the chain

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"16%, 5 + drop ml-auto"* — answering the layout question
  `[AMD-23]` put to them rather than built.

- **[READING THE INSTRUCTION]** It names two numbers that look mutually exclusive: 16 %
  gives ~6 cards at 2048 px, 5 cards needs ~20 %. **The existing code settles it.** The
  basis is already a responsive ladder with rungs at exactly 1600 px and 2000 px, so both
  numbers land as the two top rungs — `min-[1600px]:16%`, `min-[2000px]:20%` — and no
  reading of the instruction is contradicted. At the operator's own 2048 px screen that is
  5 cards, which is what "5" asked for; 16 % governs the 1600–2000 band, which is what
  "16%" asked for.

- **[DELTA]** Top two rungs `13.5% / 11%` → **`16% / 20%`**. `ml-auto` dropped at both
  `StationCard` sites, so the extra width goes to the **name** instead of stranding the
  clock at the right edge — which was the operator's stated worry and was real.

- **[WHY THIS IS NOT STYLING]** `StationCard` **truncates** the name. At 11 % a 2048 px
  screen rendered *"Yio Chu K…"* and *"Ang M…"* **in English** — visible in the operator's
  screenshot. And v0.62.815 made the names **longer**: *Stesen MRT Ang Mo Kio* is roughly
  double *Ang Mo Kio*. So at the old width, the register → join → renderer chain built
  across v0.62.813–815 would have terminated in an ellipsis. **Width was the last link, and
  without it the other three delivered nothing a reader could use.** Three versions of
  correct data, ending in "Stesen MRT Ang…".

- **[THIS REVERSES A DELIBERATE EARLIER DECISION]** The v0.6x comment in `App.jsx` argues
  for stepping the ladder **down** — it exists because a 1920 px desktop once showed the
  same ~4.5 cards as a 768 px tablet, and every extra pixel went into wider cards rather
  than more of the line. **That was right on the evidence available then.** What changed is
  that the names got longer, which that decision could not have known. The new comment sits
  **beside** the old one rather than replacing it, so the next reader sees an argument and
  its later qualification, not a silent inversion.

- **[AN ASSERTION I WROTE WRONG, AGAIN]** The first version of the width test asserted the
  ladder never narrows from `md` upward. It failed: `expected 17 to be greater than or equal
  to 22`. **The ladder is a V, not a ramp** — 60 → 31 → 22 → 17 narrows on purpose, exactly
  as the comment argues, and only the top two rungs reverse it.

  So I encoded a rule the code had never followed and the code was right. Corrected to the
  invariant that actually holds — *the widest screens get the widest cards*, a claim about
  the top rungs — with the wrong version kept in the comment. This is the second assertion
  this session written from an assumption and refuted by the artifact, after Bras Basah.
  Both times the data was correct and my expectation was invented.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `npx vite build` — clean, 468 modules. `npm test -- --run`
  → **189 files, 4,425 tests green** (+3), twice.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at v0.62.816.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **I cannot see the result.** This environment renders no browser, so the
  claim "5 cards at 2048 px" is arithmetic (2048 × 0.20 ≈ 410 px) plus the ladder's own
  structure, not an observation. The truncation fix is likewise inferred from `truncate` and
  string lengths. If the operator's screen disagrees, the numbers move in one line — and the
  map pill is still English for exactly this reason.
  Unchanged: Tamil unreachable, `LocationCard` headings hardcoded English, O-318, O-226,
  O-228, O-308, O-310 – O-313, O-184, and the unnamed failure from `[AMD-10]`. `[AMD-23]`
  and this entry ride the open #1771.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | A two-number instruction read against the existing ladder rather than picked between; both numbers implemented. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | Two class strings. The earlier decision's reasoning is preserved next to its reversal. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | The reversal is argued in place, per the repo's habit of keeping the superseded reasoning rather than deleting it. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ⚠️ | Build and tests pass, but **the visual result is unverified** — no renderer here. Stated rather than implied. |

### [AMD-25] The headings follow the names — and a guard caught me writing a CI break

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"fix the LocationCard headings too"*.

- **[DELTA]** `mrt.youAreHere` and `mrt.nearestMrt` added across **all eight locales**, and
  `LocationCard` now calls `t()` instead of carrying English literals.

  Phrasing follows the **existing `mrt.nearestHawker`** in each locale rather than being
  invented per language — *Hawker terdekat* → *MRT terdekat*, *最近的小贩中心* → *最近的地铁站*,
  *Nächster Hawker* → *Nächste MRT-Station*. The two "nearest X" labels sit next to each
  other in this app, so they should read as a pair; translating one from scratch would have
  produced a subtly different register beside its sibling.

  This closes the seam the operator was looking at. Half-translating a card is worse than
  not translating it, because the reader can **see** the join: 宏茂桥地铁站 under the words
  "Nearest MRT".

- **[X — I WROTE THE EXACT FAILURE A GUARD EXISTS TO PREVENT, AND IT CAUGHT ME]**

  My first version of the test imported `t` from `web/transport/src/i18n.js`. It passed
  here. `__tests__/test-import-graph-guard.test.js` failed it:

  > `web/transport/src/i18n.js` imports `"react"`, which the repo root cannot resolve
  > (it lives in a TMA's own `node_modules`).

  It passed **only because a TMA build had already populated `web/transport/node_modules`
  earlier in this session.** The root `npm ci` that CI's unit job runs installs no TMA
  dependencies, so this would have been green on my machine and red on the runner — which
  is what that guard's own header calls "the worst shape a failure can take", written after
  v0.62.704 shipped precisely this. I reproduced its founding case inside the file I was
  adding to close a different item.

  Rewritten to **parse the tables from source** and merge them under the module's own rule.
  Less direct, and it buys something: the parser sees keys the real merge silently
  discards, which is what made O-323 findable at all.

  **Fourth time this session a pre-existing guard has stopped me** — `prepush-guard` twice
  on merged-PR pushes, `serial-state-anchor` on the timezone slip, and now this. Each was a
  rule written down after someone paid for it once.

- **[O-323 — FOUND WHILE FIXING SOMETHING ELSE]** That module merges its six locale blocks
  with `if (STRINGS[k] && STRINGS[k].zh == null)`. The `STRINGS[k] &&` means **a locale key
  with no base entry is dropped in silence** — a typo'd Russian key, or a base key later
  renamed, leaves a translation sitting in the file that reads as coverage in a diff and can
  never render. Same orphan shape as O-317's overlay rows, different file. Now asserted
  across all six blocks; **0 orphans today**, so this is a guard against a future edit
  rather than a repair.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Both new gates **proved by mutation**: a typo'd `id` key
  fires two assertions; a deleted `zh` value fires the English-fallback one; the file was
  restored and re-diffed to confirm the mutations left nothing behind. Resolution printed
  for all eight locales before wiring. `npx vite build` clean. `npm test -- --run` →
  **190 files, 4,434 tests green**, twice.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at v0.62.817.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **Tamil is still unreachable** — no `ta` locale, and adding one without a
  Tamil UI string set trades an English interface for a worse English interface. The map
  pill is still English pending someone who can render the map. And I still cannot see any
  of this: the card layout, the heading fit, the 5-card width are all arithmetic and source
  reading, not observation.
  Unchanged: O-318, O-226, O-228, O-308, O-310 – O-313, O-184, and the unnamed
  collection-time failure from `[AMD-10]`. `[AMD-23]`, `[AMD-24]` and this entry ride #1771.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Two headings, as asked. O-323 was found on the way and filed rather than folded in silently. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | Two i18n keys and two JSX substitutions. Mutations reverted and verified reverted. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | New gates proved by mutation, per this repo's practice. The import-graph rule was obeyed rather than worked around. |

### [AMD-27] The Vibe Journal catches up 85 PRs — and the secret scan earns its keep

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"update the vibe coding record"*. The ledger's last row
  was **#1685** (1 Aug); `main` was at **#1771**. Twenty-seven days and 85 PRs of drift on a
  page served publicly at `soleat.net/doc/vibe-journal.html`.

- **[DELTA]**

  - **`data/pr-files.tsv`** — +85 rows, derived locally from
    `git log origin/main --name-only`, one row per squash commit whose subject ends `(#N)`.
  - **`data/prs.ndjson`** — +85 rows fetched from the GitHub PR API in six pages, HTML
    entities decoded and the `🤖 Generated with…` footer stripped (it is generator noise,
    not description). Matches the newest rows' conventions: full body, `state: "MERGED"`,
    `+00:00` offsets.
  - **`generate.mjs`** — `GEN_DATE` → `2026-08-28` with this catch-up appended to its
    running note, plus the `maxPr` fix below.
  - Regenerated: `records.tsv` (**1,743 rows**), `vibe-coding-record.md`,
    `public/doc/vibe-journal.html`, `public/doc/vibe-journal.json`.

  **#1707 is absent, and that is correct.** The fetch returned 90 PRs across the contiguous
  span 1681–1771 — one short of 91. That number was never a PR in this repo. It is stated
  here because "85 of 86" looks like a dropped row, and the next reader deserves the reason
  rather than the discrepancy.

- **[X — THE SECRET SCAN FOUND SOMETHING, AND IT WAS NOT MINE]** The pre-write scan matched
  three rows:

  1. **#1722's body quotes the `ASIA6languages.*` token value verbatim** — as the worked
     example of a word-shaped credential that defeats shape and entropy detection. The irony
     is total: a PR *about* redaction, carrying the thing unredacted.
  2. **#265 and #1021 carry the operator's Telegram chat id**, quoted from Railway log lines.
     Both **pre-existing** — on the hosted page since June, not introduced today.

### [AMD-29] O-324 closed by measurement — and I had the count wrong

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"do o-324"*. Yesterday's answer was *"measured,
  deliberately not retuned — the operator decides"*. The operator decided.

- **[DELTA]** `featureAreaOf` resolves in three stages, strongest evidence first:

  1. **TITLE** — the PR's own statement of what it is;
  2. **FILES** — a new `FILE_RULES` path table; what a PR actually changed cannot be
     mentioned in passing;
  3. **BODY** — the old behaviour, kept rather than removed, because **338 rows have no file
     list at all** (pre-squash PRs and a handful since).

  Two `FILE_RULES` decisions are load-bearing and easy to undo by accident, so both carry
  their reason in the source: `Docs / vault` and `Infra / setup` only win when nothing else
  scored (`doc/.serial-state.yml` rides along on nearly every PR here, so otherwise every
  feature PR files itself as documentation); and the dish corpora count as **Cuisine
  Picker**, not `Language / i18n`.

- **[X — MY OWN COUNT WAS WRONG, AND WRONG IN THE WAY THE ITEM IS ABOUT]** O-324 said **7 of
  8** rows were mis-filed. **It was 6.** #1692's file list is `web/hawker/**` — it is
  genuinely a Hawker PR. I had judged it from its title alone.

  That is the same shortcut the item exists to name: deciding what a change is about from one
  weak signal. It shipped in #1772's body and in the Register before the file list I was
  already holding contradicted it. Corrected in both places rather than quietly restated, and
  #1690 and #1692 are now **pinned as must-not-move** — a fix that moves everything is not a
  fix, and those two are the guard against one.

- **[HOW IT WAS DECIDED — measurement, not plausibility]** A **seeded 50-PR sample, labelled
  by hand from title + file list BEFORE any strategy was run against it** (the sample script
  prints only number, title and directories, so the labels could not be steered by the
  outputs they would judge):

  | Strategy | all 50 | the 42 unambiguous |
  | :--- | ---: | ---: |
  | current (one bag, first match) | 38 % | 38 % |
  | title, then body | 46 % | 48 % |
  | score areas by body keyword *frequency* | 48 % | 48 % |
  | **title → files → body** | **66 %** | **71 %** |

  The frequency variant is the instructive rejection: it looked principled, scored no better
  than title-then-body, and **stripped 219 rows off `Cuisine Picker`**. It failed
  differently, not less — which distribution-gazing alone would never have shown, and which
  is why the labels were worth the hour.

  A second measured choice inside the winner: routing the dish corpora to `Language / i18n`
  (tempting — the translation programme works on them) scored **62 %** against **66 %** for
  `Cuisine Picker`. Recorded in the source next to the rule, with the number.

- **[THE COST, STATED RATHER THAN DISCOVERED]** **799 rows moved — 45.8 % of the ledger.**
  Churn-by-area and fix-density are **not comparable with any earlier snapshot**. That was my
  objection to doing this yesterday; it is still true, it is now the operator's call, and it
  is written into `VibeCodingRecord.md` where a reader of the page will meet it.

  Not every move is an improvement, but the sample says the balance is: `Oversight / usage
  stats` fell 22 → 5, and reading the 17 that left, they include *"visible dark-mode borders
  across all 5 TMAs"* and *"URGENT iPad Pro header"* — rows that matched the bare word
  `oversight` in a body listing the five apps. That drop is a correction.

- **[O-326 — FOUND WHILE FIXING O-324]** 116 rows (#1552–#1665) carried `n` as a **string**.
  A lookup keyed on a number missed them, which is the only reason it surfaced. Nothing
  internal ever broke — every use already wrapped it in `Number()`, and the table's sort
  coerces with `+x` — which is precisely why it survived months. The damage was outward:
  `vibe-journal.json` is offered as the file to feed to `jq` / DuckDB / pandas, and a mixed
  `int`/`str` column breaks a join there silently. Normalised in the data **and** coerced at
  read, so a future dump that quotes the number cannot reintroduce it.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `__tests__/vibe-journal-feature-area.test.js` — 13 tests,
  reading the **generated** `records.tsv` and `vibe-journal.json` rather than importing the
  generator (importing it writes four files as a side effect; and the artefact is what is
  published, so a green test over a function whose output never reached the page would be the
  wrong gate). **Both gates proved by mutation:** reverting `featureAreaOf` to the one-bag
  version fires **exactly the six** probe assertions that were wrong and leaves #1690 and
  #1692 green; putting the dish corpora back on `i18n` fires the routing assertion. Generator
  re-run and the sample re-scored **against the shipped artefact**, not the experiment —
  66 % / 71 %, identical to the harness. `npm test -- --run` → **191 files, 4,447 tests green**.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at v0.62.819.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **10 of the 17 residual sample misses are one taxonomy question**, not a
  bug: is a map fix inside the Cuisine TMA `Maps / geo / location` or `Cuisine Picker`? Rules
  cannot settle that — the taxonomy has to, and that needs the operator. `Maps / geo /
  location` holds 21 rows, which either means the app has little map work (false) or means
  that question is being answered "Cuisine Picker" by default.

  The labels are **mine**, 50 of 1,743, and 8 were marked arguable before scoring. A second
  labeller would not agree with all of them. The 38 → 66 gap is far larger than that noise,
  but the absolute numbers should not be read as precision.

  Unchanged: O-325 (**the token still needs rotating in Railway — only the operator can**),
  Tamil unreachable, the map pill, `session-replies.ndjson` at the 22-08 extraction by
  choice, O-318, O-226, O-228, O-308, O-310 – O-313, O-184.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | O-324 as filed. O-326 was found on the way and closed with it rather than left for a reader to trip over. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ⚠️ | No external calls. But **799 rows of published analysis changed meaning** — reversible (one function), and the magnitude is stated in the file, the Register and the PR rather than buried. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | Decided by measurement against blind hand labels, per this repo's rule that a heuristic is judged by measured error and not by plausibility. Rejected alternatives recorded with their numbers. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | The sample was re-scored against the **shipped** `records.tsv`, not the experiment harness, and the two agreed exactly. |

### [AMD-31] A coverage audit, and three items filed — none of them a hole

- **[INTENT]** Operator: *"are all translations recorded in the table? all michelin data
  recorded"*, then *"file all three"*. The audit ran first; these are the three things it
  turned up that were worth a Register number.

- **[WHAT THE AUDIT MEASURED]** Every corpus by **evaluating the module**, never by grepping —
  a grep counts text, and the question is what the app resolves to at render time.

  | Corpus | Rows | At all 8 locales |
  | :--- | ---: | :--- |
  | `classics-notes` | 1,672 | **1,672** · 0 orphans |
  | nation-overlay dish notes | 1,653 | **1,653** |
  | `city-plates` (126 cities) | 279 | **279** · 0 orphans |
  | bot `i18n.js` | 276 keys | 2,112 of 2,208; 96 absent **by design**, all pinned |
  | Cuisine / Sketchbook / Transport / Hawker TMAs | 232 / 242 / 120 / 75 | complete |
  | Menu TMA | 71 | **69** — see O-327 |
  | MRT station table | 193 | zh 193 · ms 193 · ta 193; all **183** live stations |

  Michelin, two datasets that are separate on purpose (a test asserts it): `michelin-data.js`
  **2,118 venues / 11 countries / 61 cities** — 2026 awards 2,099, 2025 627; Bib 1,542, 1★ 830,
  2★ 262, 3★ 92, 8 Green Star — and `SG-michelin.js` **148** (3★ 3, 2★ 9, 1★ 37, Bib 99).
  **The one real hole is O-228, and it measures exactly as filed: 101 venues with no cuisine,
  every one of them Paris.** Nothing else in the 2,118 is missing one.

- **[X — I REPORTED "FRENCH: 0" AND IT WAS A FACT ABOUT WHERE I LOOKED]** The first pass
  measured the overlay alone and announced that `classics-notes` had **no French at all** —
  1,672 notes, zero `fr`. It was wrong: `en` and `fr` live in the **base** file's `note`
  object, and the overlay carries only the six later locales. Caught before it reached the
  operator, by the reflex of checking a row's actual shape when a number looks that dramatic.

  The same reflex caught two more would-be findings: **three venues with an empty `awards[]`**
  (all three are Green Star entries, tracked separately) and **Singapore missing from
  `COUNTRY_MANIFEST`** (deliberate — `SG-michelin.js` is a separate dataset with its own
  shape, and `michelin-available-cuisines.test.js` asserts `availableCuisines('SG')` is `[]`
  with the reason in a comment). Three near-misses in one audit, all the same shape: an
  absence that is a design decision I had not read yet.

- **[DELTA — three Register items, none of them fixed]**

  - **O-327** — the Menu TMA's two correct non-translations (`'Sketchbook'`, `'Soleat'`) with
    nothing to distinguish them from an oversight. The bot solves exactly this with 96 pinned
    absences and a test that fails on a stale pin *or* an unpinned one; no TMA has that.
  - **O-328** — 32 orphaned overlay rows (19 `georgian::`, 13 `japanese::`). **Verified on all
    32, not on a probe: every one of those dishes is in `classics-notes` and translated at all
    eight locales.** Stale duplication, zero coverage loss.
  - **O-329** — Bukit Panjang, Choa Chu Kang, Punggol and Sengkang each hold two rows (MRT and
    LRT). The `Map` keeps the last; all four currently resolve to **MRT**, which is right —
    but by sort order, not by design, and nothing tests it.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Every figure above came from `require()`ing or parsing the
  actual module. The O-328 claim was re-run across all 32 rows rather than the four I first
  probed. `npm test -- --run` → **191 files, 4,447 tests green**.

### [AMD-33] O-327 and O-329 fixed — both were "correct by accident"

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"fix o-327 and o-329"*. Not O-328, which is the one whose
  fix belongs to a generator this repo has already filed a separate item about.

- **[WHAT THEY HAD IN COMMON]** Neither was producing a wrong answer. The Menu TMA's two
  absences are correct, and all four duplicate stations already resolved to MRT. **Both were
  right for reasons nothing recorded and nothing tested** — the failure mode this repo keeps
  meeting, and the same shape as O-317's case-sensitive join and O-323's silent overlay drop.

- **[DELTA — O-327]** `__tests__/tma-i18n-coverage.test.js`, **24 tests**, the bot's pinning
  contract applied to **all five** Mini Apps in one file rather than five copies:

  - an absence must be translated **or** pinned with a reason from a **closed set**;
  - every pin is checked to be **still absent**, so a stale exemption fails;
  - every base entry must have an English root to fall back to;
  - **O-323 generalised** — the silent-drop check ran on Transport only; it now runs on
    Cuisine, Hawker and Menu too. **0 orphans in all four.**

  The two Menu absences are pinned `proper-noun`: *Sketchbook* is a named surface of this
  product and *Soleat* is the product. Translating either renames the thing rather than
  localising it.

  **[X — THREE PARSERS, ALL CONFIDENTLY WRONG]** Writing this test meant parsing five files
  in two shapes, and I got it wrong three times, each time with a number that looked
  authoritative:

  1. `key: { … },` missed the **last** entry in each block (no trailing comma) and any
     multi-line value. It reported `sheet.dragHandle`, `rating.resetBody` and
     `localeToggle.close` as **orphans in three more apps** — I was one step from filing
     three new Register items about translations that are perfectly fine.
  2. A line-anchored key regex alone cannot tell an inline 8-locale entry from a base pair,
     so Sketchbook read as **zero locale coverage** when it has all eight inline.
  3. Only slicing each entry from its own key line to the **next** key line held.

  What saved it was checking the file rather than trusting the parser — `grep sheet.dragHandle`
  takes five seconds and showed a base entry sitting at line 189. The first assertion in the
  finished test is now that the parser finds exactly those three awkward keys, because a
  broken parser passes every other assertion in the file.

- **[DELTA — O-329]** `SG_STATION_NAMES_BY_NAME` and `BY_FOLD` are built by one `_indexBy`
  that **prefers the `MRT` row explicitly**. Both indexes, deliberately: fixing one and not
  the other would have looked fixed and behaved differently through `stationRow('punggol')`.

  Five assertions added: the **premise** (those four names do carry two rows each, and
  nothing else is doubled — so the pinned list is complete), the preference through the exact
  index, the same through the folded index, and that 193 − 189 = 4.

  **And the header's own arithmetic was wrong.** It said *"the extra 10 are stations
  announced but not yet in SG_STATIONS"* and then listed **six**. The real sum is
  **183 live + 6 announced + 4 duplicate names = 193**. The missing four were these
  interchanges all along — the comment had subtracted two numbers, listed whichever names
  came to mind, and read as measured. Corrected in place with the sum spelled out.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** **Four mutations, four gates:** preferring LRT fires five
  station assertions; dropping a pin fires the unpinned-absence assertion; giving a pinned key
  a translation fires the stale-pin assertion; adding a typo'd key to a locale block fires the
  orphan assertion. Every file restored and re-diffed afterwards. The regenerator
  (`scripts/fetch-mrt-station-names.mjs`) splits on a line that is exactly `];` and there is
  still exactly one, so the new lookup code survives a regeneration — checked, not assumed.
  `npm test -- --run` → **192 files, 4,478 tests green**.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at v0.62.820.

- **[TEST]** +24 (`tma-i18n-coverage`) and +5 (`mrt-stations-i18n`), 4,447 → 4,478.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **O-328 is deliberately not fixed** — 32 orphaned dish-note overlay rows.
  Deleting them is trivial; the regenerator should stop producing them, and **O-313** already
  records that `scripts/translate-content.mjs` does not know `classics-notes.js` or
  `city-plates.js`. Fixing the symptom here would make the real item look done.

### [AMD-35] O-318 closed as a gate; O-325 closed on the operator's word; O-330 found on the first run

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"do o-318, close O-325"*.

- **[O-318 — THE METHOD IS THE SUBSTANCE]** The item existed because a rule that looked
  obviously right scored **38 flags, 37 of them legitimate** — including three where the
  regex read *"served over turkey"* (the bird) as Turkish. It asked for a **reliable
  discriminator** and said it would otherwise stay open as a known limit.

  There is no rule that reads provenance out of one note's English. There did not have to be.
  **1,461 keys exist in both `classics-notes.js` and `nation-overlay-dishnotes.generated.js`,
  independently authored**, and the row's own scope is a third fixed fact. So the flag became:

  > the classics note names regions, **none** of them is the row's own scope, **and** the
  > sibling note for the same key **does** name the scope.

  Two strings disagreeing about a third — no judgement about English anywhere in it. It is
  also the shape that caught the fourth O-315 row by hand, when a reader noticed the overlay
  said *"northeastern China"* where classics said *"Cantonese … postwar Hong Kong"*.

  **Measured at every step, because that is what the item was about:**

  | Rule | Flags |
  | :--- | ---: |
  | one note's prose (the rejected rule) | 38, of which 37 legitimate |
  | two notes disagree on region | 16 — **six of them homonyms** |
  | homonyms fixed | 10 |
  | + the scope arbitrates | **8** |

  The six homonyms are worth naming because they are the same disease O-318 documented:
  *"a deep green **hue**"* → Vietnamese, *"**baba** ghanoush"* → Peranakan, *"a **New
  England** soup"* → British. The vocabulary now asserts against all four in its own test,
  so the next person to widen a pattern finds out immediately.

- **[EIGHT IS NOT ZERO — SO IT SHIPS AS A RULE PLUS A REGISTER]** The remaining eight are
  correct notes whose foreign region is an **origin**, an **etymology**, an **ingredient** or
  a **method** — *"Cantonese-origin egg noodles"*, *"the name traces to the
  Kristang/Portuguese 'bolu'"*, *"brewed Turkish-style"*, *"on French bread"*. That is exactly
  the distinction no regex drew; it is now written down once, per row, with a reason from a
  closed set. **A ninth fails CI.** Same contract as the bot's 96 pinned i18n absences and
  O-317's pinned 23 — this repo's answer to a rule that is useful but not clean.

  **What it would have caught:** 2 of O-315's 4, measured by running it against the
  pre-removal corpus. A third is already caught by the protein-vs-endonym gate. **The fourth,
  `roast suckling pig`, is invisible to both** — Latin-script endonym *feng*, and its note
  names no region at all. Written into the test header so the gate is not read as wider than
  it is.

- **[O-330 — THE GATE FOUND SOMETHING ON ITS FIRST RUN]** `eurasian::soyok`. Its own note
  reads *"Not a verified food or drink; in Malay 'soyok' is an architectural term for a
  roofed lean-to or verandah extension of a traditional house."* It is sourced — correctly,
  to a Malay dictionary — and translated into all eight locales. And, **checked rather than
  assumed** per O-317's lesson: `soyok` **is** in the eurasian overlay's `iconicDishes`, so a
  reader who opens the Eurasian plate can be shown a card explaining a type of verandah.

  Not fixed. Removing a dish from a plate changes what the app offers, which is the operator's
  call — the same treatment O-315's four refusals and O-317's 23 unreachable notes got. It is
  pinned in the new gate as `open-defect` rather than as an exemption, so CI stays honest
  instead of green by omission.

- **[O-325 — CLOSED ON INSTRUCTION, AND THE TWO HALVES ARE NOT THE SAME KIND OF FACT]** The
  operator said to close it, and it is theirs to close. What that closure rests on is worth
  separating:

  - **Verified by content, twice** — the token and the chat id are redacted in
    `data/prs.ndjson` and absent from all five generated artefacts, re-scanned after
    regeneration and again from the merged tree; and `VibeCodingRecord.md` carries a mandatory
    redaction step so a re-dump cannot reintroduce them.
  - **Not verifiable from here** — whether `I18N_TRANSLATE_TOKEN` has actually been rotated in
    Railway. This container has no Railway credentials and never held the token. Per **D-199**,
    repo state is evidence about the past; only the live service is authority on the current
    key. Recorded as the operator's attestation, not as a measurement.

  The bodies on GitHub are unchanged and public, so **if the rotation has not happened the
  exposure stands and the item should be reopened.** That sentence is in the Register too,
  because a closure that hides which half was checked is worse than an open item.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** **Three mutations, three gates:** rewriting
  `japanese::chirashi don` as a Cantonese/Hong Kong dish fires the unpinned-flag assertion
  (`expected [ 'japanese::chirashi don' ] to deeply equal []`); dropping a pin fires it for
  `lebanese::lebanese coffee`; adding a pin for a non-contradiction fires the stale-pin
  assertion. `classics-notes.js` restored and re-diffed clean after each. The homonym
  assertions are their own test rather than a comment. `npm test -- --run` →
  **193 files, 4,484 tests green**.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at v0.62.821.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **O-330 is open and reader-visible** — the one-line fix is dropping `soyok`
  from `iconicDishes` and removing its note plus overlay row, and it needs the operator.
  **O-328** still open by decision (the regenerator owns it, via O-313). O-226 and O-228 need
  a source, not code. Tamil unreachable, the map pill, `session-replies.ndjson` at the 22-08
  extraction by choice, O-308, O-310 – O-313, O-184.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | :--- | :--- | :--- |

### [AMD-37] O-330 closed — and execution found three sources describing soyok three ways

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"O-330"*, answering the offer to remove `eurasian::soyok`.

- **[DELTA]** Removed from **all five** places it lived, not one:

  | File | What it held |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | `nation-overlay.js` | the `F('soyok')` entry in eurasian `iconicDishes`, carrying its own inline 8-locale note |
  | `classics-notes.js` | the *"Not a verified food or drink"* row |
  | `classics-notes-i18n.generated.js` | its six-locale overlay row |
  | `nation-overlay-dishnotes.generated.js` | a second English note |
  | `nation-overlay-dishnotes-i18n.generated.js` | that note's seven locales |

  **Removing only the classics row would have been worse than leaving it.** `index.js` merges
  `{ ...d, ...m }`, so the classics note *overwrites* the overlay's. Deleting it alone would
  have swapped *"not a verified food"* for an unverified description of a salad — a quieter
  wrong answer, not a fix. Checked before editing, because O-315's removals worked the
  opposite way: there, deleting the classics row **restored** a correct overlay note.

- **[X — EXECUTION CHANGED THE REASON, AND STRENGTHENED IT]** I opened O-330 on one ground:
  a note that says its dish is not a dish. Removing it surfaced a better one. **Three places
  in this repo describe soyok three different ways:**

  1. `classics-notes` — *"Not a verified food or drink; in Malay 'soyok' is an architectural
     term for a roofed lean-to"*, sourced to a Malay dictionary;
  2. `nation-overlay-dishnotes` — *"a Eurasian salad made with pork offal, fresh cucumbers,
     and herbs like galangal and lemongrass"*, sourced to Wonderwall.sg;
  3. `nation-overlay.js`, in its own inline comment on the line: `// Eurasian soya pork`.

  Three accounts, three dishes, one of them not a dish at all. **The corpus cannot say what
  soyok is.** That is a better reason to retire the row than the one the item was opened on,
  and it is worth saying plainly that I found it *while executing an approved instruction*
  rather than while arguing for it — the sourced Wonderwall.sg claim is real evidence that
  something called soyok may be a Eurasian dish, and it did not survive contact with the other
  two accounts. Anyone who finds the real answer can restore this from the verbatim record
  below.

### [AMD-39] O-228 closed — the two-day blocker was a missing User-Agent

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"O-226 / O-228"*. Both items said the same thing: *clears
  when the official per-venue listing becomes fetchable*. So the work was to find out whether
  it is.

- **[X — IT WAS FETCHABLE ALL ALONG]** `FR-michelin.js`'s header recorded that
  guide.michelin.com *"returns HTTP 403 to server-side fetch"* and deferred one-star and Bib
  Gourmand to "a browser-verified follow-up". **The same request carrying an ordinary desktop
  User-Agent returns 200 and a fully server-rendered card list** — name, city, price, `·`,
  cooking style, all in the HTML. A deferral that shaped two Register items and a file's scope
  note stood for two days on a cause one header removed.

  The old note is **kept beside the correction**, not rewritten. What it got wrong is the
  useful part: "403" was read as a policy and it was a handshake.

- **[DELTA — O-228]** Eight listing pages across the three star tiers, joined to our rows by
  an accent- and punctuation-folded **exact** match. **98 of 101 filled.**
  `availableCuisines('FR')` goes **3 → 12**; the corpus drops from 101 uncuisined rows to 3.

  **A substring pass was written and thrown away**, and it is worth recording what it did:
  it joined `Pages` and `Le Violon d'Ingres` to a card called **ES** — because "es" is inside
  both — and equated `La Scène Thélème` with `La Scène`, which is a different restaurant.
  Three wrong joins out of thirteen. **The same class of error as O-317's case-sensitivity
  and O-329's sort-order win**: a match that looks right because nothing checked it. Exact
  fold plus four rows matched **by name** (Table, Pur, Gaya, Pavyllon — MICHELIN prints the
  chef or the city in their full names) is what shipped.

  **Three rows are still empty and are named, not counted** — Galanga, Pages, La Scène
  Thélème. They hold 2026 one-stars here, appear in none of the eight pages, and their guessed
  detail-page slugs 404. Pinned by name in the new test so filling one shrinks the list.

- **[DELTA — O-226, NARROWED NOT CLOSED]** The item offered two explanations for the missing
  Indonesian label. **The primary source eliminates the first.** The MICHELIN ceremony article
  reads: *"Shenzhen's debut Selection features 21 restaurants awarded the Bib Gourmand,
  covering over 10 cooking styles including Cantonese, Chao Zhou, Sichuan, Hakka, Hunan, Shun
  Tak, and Indonesian."* That is the **Bib tier**, not the whole selection.

  **And it is two labels, not one:** of the seven styles named, our 21 rows carry Cantonese,
  Chao Zhou, Hakka, Hunan and Shun Tak — but neither **Sichuan** nor **Indonesian**.

  **[X — I NEARLY REPORTED A ROSTER MISMATCH THAT DOES NOT EXIST.]** A web-search summary
  presented Baode, Si Mao Cai Guan and others as Shenzhen's Bib venues; none is in our 21, and
  for a moment that looked like a much bigger problem. Reading **the article's own paragraph**
  rather than the summary showed they are **Guangzhou's 8**. The summary was not wrong so much
  as flattened, and flattening is what a summary does — which is why the paragraph got read.

  Shenzhen's listing is still not fetchable: its path returns **Hong Kong** cards server-side
  on every page and filter tried, so the UA fix that unblocked Paris does not reach it. Open.

- **[O-331 — THE COST OF CLOSING O-228, FILED RATHER THAN HIDDEN]** Paris now carries two
  cuisine vocabularies: 26 rows say `french-contemporary` / `french-classic`, 98 say
  `modern-cuisine` / `creative` / `classic-cuisine`. MICHELIN's own site calls Kei *Modern
  Cuisine* and Arpège *Creative*, where this repo had written `french-contemporary` for both.

  Mapping the site's labels onto the curated ones was rejected **on this repo's own
  precedent** — it would turn a copy of a register into a derived table, the same ruling made
  about *Stesen* vs *Stasiun*. And it measured worse: the rest of the corpus already uses the
  slugified site labels, so copying them leaves **26** as the outlier instead of **98**.
  Which way to reconcile is a ruling that changes what a filter returns, so it is the
  operator's.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `__tests__/michelin-paris-cuisine.test.js` — 6 tests, and it
  **does not re-fetch**: a gate that needs the network is a gate that fails on a bad
  afternoon. It pins the count (124 of 127), the three named empties, the 12 FR slugs, and
  that every filled cuisine is a `kebab()` of a MICHELIN label. Both gates proved by mutation:
  stripping one cuisine fires the floor *and* the named-empties assertion; inventing
  `french-modern` fires the slug-count *and* the vocabulary assertion. `npm test -- --run` →
  **194 files, 4,490 tests green**.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at v0.62.823.

- **[TEST]** +6. 4,484 → 4,490.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **O-226 open** — needs a Shenzhen listing that renders Shenzhen, or a
  per-venue source naming which two of the 21 are Sichuan and Indonesian. **O-331 open** — a
  ruling, not code. **Three Paris rows** still uncuisined, named. O-328 open by decision
  (O-313 owns it). Tamil, the map pill, `session-replies.ndjson` by choice, O-308,
  O-310 – O-313, O-184.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Both items worked; one closed, one narrowed with its reason. O-331 filed rather than folded in as a silent side effect. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ⚠️ | Read-only public fetches, no spend on any keyed API. **98 data rows gained a field** — additive, reversible, and sourced to the page they came from. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | The substring join was measured and discarded before it could ship, per this repo's rule that a matcher is judged by its false positives. |

### [AMD-41] Phase 1 — 1,202 curated native names that had been read from disk and dropped

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"phase 1"*, following the drafted plan and the inquiry
  behind it: *"Do you think translating eateries into the local language of selection … as a
  second line in bracket will be helpful?"* Phase 1 is the half that needs no ruling.

- **[THE FINDING, WHICH IS NOT A TRANSLATION]** `JP-michelin.js`, `CN-michelin.js` and
  `KR-michelin.js` already carry `nameJa` / `nameZh` / `nameKo` and their address twins on
  **1,202 rows**, hand-curated. `venueToVenue()` rebuilds every venue from a **field
  whitelist**, and those fields were not on it — loaded and discarded in the same breath.
  Meanwhile **both renderers for them shipped in v0.61.359 and have been waiting since**:
  `venue-templates.js:217-220` emits `(nameLocal)` under the name in chat, and
  `ResultCard.jsx:438` emits the same row in the Mini App. **Nothing was translated in this
  change and no external API was called.** A curated register moved one field to the left.

- **[DELTA]** `michelin-data.js` — `nativeName` / `nativeAddr` normalised onto `nameLocal` /
  `addressLocal` (plus `postal`, which the whitelist was dropping too). Measured after:
  **`nameLocal` 1,202 · `addressLocal` 1,186 · JP 590 / CN 495 / KR 117 · leaked into a
  Latin-script country: 0 · corpus still 2,118.**

  **`name` is untouched, on purpose, and that is the first test in the file rather than the
  last.** `venue.name` is not only shown: it is the Google Maps query (`ResultCard.jsx:176`),
  the `detailsId` DOM selector (`:215`), and the clipboard and share payloads (`:227`, `:243`).
  A localised string in that slot breaks a **key**, not a label — saved items orphan, the
  carousel stops finding its card, Maps searches for kanji, and nothing throws. The station
  work came within one field of shipping exactly that (O-329, and O-317 before it).

- **[X — MY OWN GATE'S CHARACTER CLASS WAS THE BUG, AND IT FOUND A REAL ONE]** The key
  assertion flagged one row: `Suyab Courtyard・Pickmoon Gourmet`. CN source rows whose `name`
  holds an actual ideograph measure **0**, so the flag was false — my class spanned `぀-ヿ`,
  which contains **U+30FB KATAKANA MIDDLE DOT**, punctuation used as a separator inside a
  romanised name.

  **The same span is in production.** `translate-name.js`'s `RE_KANA` is that literal range, so
  `nameScriptLang('Suyab Courtyard・Pickmoon Gourmet')` returned `'ja'`; in a CN search that is
  `'ja' !== 'zh'`, which sends an already-readable Latin name to the LLM for a reading — **a
  paid call and a wrong 🔤 line**. Fixed there, with the range written out and the two marks
  that matter kept (U+309D `ゝ`, which `祇園 さゝ木` needs, and U+30FC `ー`). **Five other copies
  of the span are left standing and listed by file in O-332**, each needing its own
  measurement; a blanket replace across six regexes is the move this repo has twice recorded
  as wrong.

  Worth stating plainly: **an assertion I wrote to catch a data defect caught a code defect
  instead, and only because it was written to fail loudly rather than to pass.**

- **[VERIFIED, NOT ASSUMED — that the change reaches a screen and costs nothing]**
  `cuisine-stream.js` spreads venues wholesale (`{ ...v }`), so there is no second whitelist
  between the loader and the card. `local-name.js`'s `attachLocalNames` **skips any venue that
  already carries `nameLocal`** — so the 1,202 cannot trigger a Places fetch. And
  `attachNameReadings` returns early on a Latin name, measured: of the 1,202, the number whose
  `name` `nameScriptLang` reads as non-Latin is **1** — the middle-dot row above, which is now
  0. **No new spend on any keyed API, in either direction.**

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `__tests__/michelin-native-name.test.js` — **14 tests**, ordered
  so the display-versus-key invariant runs first. It asserts the names byte-match their source
  rows (a sample of 30, `nameLocal` and `addressLocal` both), that the total matches what the
  country tables themselves hold rather than only itself, and that both renderers still behave
  — including that a venue with **no** native name renders with no empty brackets. **CN is
  pinned at 495 of 511, not 511**: sixteen rows genuinely carry no `nameZh`, and a test pinned
  at 511 would fail on correct data, which is how a guard teaches the next person to weaken it.

  **Three mutations, each fired:** stripping one `nameJa` from the source failed 3 assertions
  (the total, the JP count, the renderer); writing the native string into `name` failed 4,
  including the two key gates; filling `nameLocal` for every row failed 7, including the
  Latin-country leak gate and the byte-match. In `translate-name.test.js`, widening the kana
  class back over U+30FB fires; and the iteration/prolonged-mark cases are asserted **beside
  Han and nothing else**, because beside kana they prove nothing — `ヴィーガン` stays `ja` on `ヴ`
  alone, so the first draft of that test passed under a mutation that removed both marks.

  `npm test -- --run` → **195 files, 4,508 tests green** (4,490 → +14 new file, +4
  `translate-name`).

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at **v0.62.824**. Carries `[AMD-40]` forward per **D-205**.

- **[TEST]** +18. 4,490 → 4,508.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **O-333 — Phase 2 is a ruling, not code.** A `ja` reader in Tokyo sees
  `Gion Sasaki` above `(祇園 さゝ木)`: the romanisation leading, the name they actually use
  bracketed underneath. RULE B exists for exactly that redundancy and **does not fire here** —
  it gates the *Places fetch*, and `attachLocalNames` skips rows that already have
  `nameLocal`, so these bypass it. The swap is two render sites; it was not taken because
  reordering a line is cheap to argue about and the field carry-across is what had been broken
  for three versions.

  **O-334 — 596 venues in non-Latin-script countries still carry no native name**: TW 219 +
  HK 147 + MO 34 (no `nameZh` in those tables at all), TH 180, and the 16 CN rows. Curating is
  free and slow; the Places route spends per venue per search and returns **Traditional**
  Chinese for TW/HK/MO, a different string from the Simplified the CN table holds.

  **O-332 — five more copies of the punctuation-spanning kana class**, named by file.

  Carried: O-226, O-331, O-328 by decision, O-325's half-attested closure, Tamil, the map
  pill, `session-replies.ndjson` by choice, O-308, O-310 – O-313, O-184.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Phase 1 only. The two decisions the plan reserved for the operator were filed as O-333 / O-334, not taken quietly while adjacent code was open. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | Additive fields on 1,202 rows; no external call, and the two paths that *could* spend were checked to skip these venues rather than assumed to. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | Every new assertion proved by mutation before landing — including a redraft after one mutation revealed a test that could not see it. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | Counts measured from the loaded module and cross-checked against the source tables; the false CJK flag was resolved by measuring the CN rows, not by reasoning about the regex. |

### [AMD-43] The Japanese was already written — four copies of one list stood in front of it

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"take ① and ② in one PR"*, after asking of a screenshot
  of the Cuisine TMA toggled to Japanese: *"do you think the TMA title and command info in
  foreign languages when i switch over"*. ① was the English opening-hours line; ② the two
  strings with no key.

- **[THE SHAPE OF IT]** The card read **`Closed today · Opens Sun 11:30 AM`**.
  `open-hours.js` returns **`本日休業 · 日 11:30 開店`** and has carried all eight locales for
  versions. Nothing needed translating. **Four hand-copied locale lists stood between the
  reader and a string already in the repo**, and the shortest of them came first:

  | Where | What it did |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | `index.js:14874` — `POST /api/cuisine/user-language` | rejected `ja`/`zh`/`es` with **400 `unsupported lang`** |
  | `:15547` — cuisine route body-lang | the same short five |
  | `:18319` — free-text route body-lang | the same short five again |
  | `:10413` — the Michelin path | `const ohLang = csLang === 'fr' ? 'fr' : 'en'` |

  **The first one is the whole answer to the screenshot.** That gate sits *in front of*
  `setUserLang`, which already validates against `user-prefs`' own `SUPPORTED` — a guard in
  front of a guard, where only the outer one had gone stale. A reader picking Japanese got a
  400, the choice never persisted, hydration reverted it, and **the server never learned the
  language at all**. The chrome looked Japanese because that overlay is client-side; every
  server-rendered string on the same screen was English. Two halves of one card, disagreeing,
  and each half was behaving correctly.

- **[AND THE LAST ONE IS O-305's SHAPE]** `cuisine-enrich.js:100` — the *other* search path —
  wrote `ctx.csLang || 'en'` and was right the whole time. One datum, two call sites, one of
  them forgot. The third time this repo has recorded that exact failure.

- **[X — THE ROOT CAUSE IS A COMMENT, AND IT IS KEPT RATHER THAN REWRITTEN]** `user-prefs.js`
  says *"chat replies still resolve to en/fr (i18n.js carries en/fr strings only)"*. True when
  written; **false since v0.62.511**, and measured today at **~258 keys in each of the eight**
  in `i18n.js`. Four separate authors clipped their list to five because a comment told them
  the rest was pointless. Per **AU-1** the sentence stays and an amendment sits under it —
  deleting it would hide why the copies existed, and **D-199** is exactly this: repo text is
  evidence about the past.

- **[DELTA]**
  - `open-hours.js` — `OH_LANGS` and `ohLang()`, **derived from the phrase table** rather than
    hand-listed. Callers ask; nobody re-lists. That is what stops a fifth copy.
  - `index.js` ×4 — all three gates read `SUPPORTED` from `user-prefs`; the Michelin path calls
    `ohLangFor(csLang)`.
  - `user-prefs.js` — the stale comment corrected in place, not deleted.
  - `web/cuisine/.../i18n.js` — **`header.appTitle`**, base + all six overlays. The `<h1>` had
    been the bare literal `Cuisine`: 970 keys in that table and the app's own name was not one.
    Every rendering is **lifted from the `Cuisine & filters` empty state already in `App.jsx`**
    (`料理とフィルター` → `料理`, `Кухня и фильтры` → `Кухня`), not translated fresh — the word
    was already decided per locale and simply never reused.
  - `App.jsx` ×3 — the `<h1>` keyed; the Michelin folio pill and the criteria popup both now
    print `cat.michelinBib`, which already carried `ミシュラン · ビブグルマン` / `米其林 · 必比登`
    / `Мишлен · Биб Гурман` and is what `CuisineDrawer` prints for the same chip.

- **[THE SECOND SITE WAS FOUND BY THE ASSERTION, NOT BY READING]** `.not.toContain('return
  michelinRemaining.label;')` failed twice: once on my own comment quoting the old line, and
  once on a **real second occurrence at `App.jsx:5801`**, in the criteria popup. That popup
  also read `cuisineNameBySlug.get(slug)` raw — the English catalogue name — while the pill a
  few components up passed the same value through `cuisineName()`. **69 Japanese cuisine names
  sat one function call away**, unused. Both fixed. The comment case was handled by stripping
  comment lines before the check rather than rewording the comment until the test went quiet.

- **[A CLAIM I NEARLY MADE AND DID NOT]** A first parse said `cuisine-i18n.js`'s `NAMES` held
  **7** entries, which would have made the criteria fix cosmetic and the real work a data job.
  The regex was wrong about indentation: it holds **69**, exactly the catalogue's 69. Measured
  again before deciding, and the decision reversed.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `__tests__/search-locale-threading.test.js` — 9 tests. It
  asserts the **shape**, not the strings: that `OH_LANGS` and `SUPPORTED` agree, that no
  five-locale list survives in `index.js`, that each gate names the owning module, and — as
  values rather than claims — the four rendered labels, plus that **no locale silently equals
  English** (a table that fell back would pass "returns a string" and fail this). **Five
  mutations, each fired:** the `fr`/`en` ternary restored; the five-list restored; `ja` dropped
  from the phrase table (3 assertions); `header.appTitle: '料理'` deleted; one pill reverted.
  O-327's own gate fired on the new key (cuisine 232 → 233) and the count moved **with its
  reason**, per the corpus-floor convention. `npm test -- --run` → **196 files, 4,517 tests
  green**; `web/cuisine` rebuilt.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at **v0.62.825**. Carries `[AMD-42]` forward per **D-205**.

- **[TEST]** +9. 4,508 → 4,517.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **O-337 — left standing on purpose, and the reason is spend.** Three more
  `fr`/`en` ternaries survive in the Michelin path: the place-cache key, the Places
  `languageCode`, and the enrichment cache key. Widening them gives each locale its own cache
  namespace, and every first read in a new locale is a **billed** Places call or LLM
  generation. O-335's four cost nothing because the strings were already local; these buy
  theirs. Which locales are worth paying for is the operator's call.

  **O-336** — `NAMES` covers all 69 cuisines in fr/zh/ja/es and **none** in id/ru/de, so those
  three still read English cuisine names. 207 strings; a translation job, not a wiring one.

  **O-338** — the `おすすめ:` row's `french signature plate` is force-fill composed in JS, not
  a missing translation. Recommendation recorded: **suppress it, do not localise it** — a
  fabricated recommendation reads as authoritative once it is in the reader's own language,
  and O-315 and O-330 both already ruled that way.

  Carried: O-333, O-334, O-332, O-226, O-331, O-328 by decision, O-325's half-attested
  closure, Tamil, the map pill, O-308, O-310 – O-313, O-184.

### [AMD-44] O-338 — the fabricated dish is removed at source, and it had been outliving the render

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"do o-338"* — the item filed one entry ago from their own
  screenshot, where a Japanese card read `🍲 おすすめ: french signature plate • french chef's
  recommendation`.

- **[DELTA]** The three templates in `index.js`'s v0.60.153 force-fill are gone:
  ``${label} signature plate``, ``${label} chef's recommendation``, `"Chef's choice"`. The
  removed block is kept **verbatim in a comment per AU-1** — reversibility is the point, and
  whoever disagrees should not need archaeology.

- **[WHY REMOVAL AND NOT A FILTER]** A filter was the tempting fix and it is the weaker one.
  `filterDishNames` — the module whose whole job is rejecting non-dishes — was run over all
  four boilerplate strings and **accepted every one**. Filtering would mean maintaining a
  denylist against text this repo generates itself. Deleting the generator is smaller and
  cannot drift. The measurement is **pinned as a test**, not asserted in prose, so if the
  filter ever gets stricter the next reader sees the premise change rather than a stale claim.

- **[THIS REVERSES HALF AN OPERATOR INSTRUCTION, AND THE CODE SAYS SO]** v0.60.153 asked that
  *"cards never ship empty"*. Some Michelin cards will now show **no `🍲` row at all**. That is
  the correct empty state and it was checked rather than hoped: `ResultCard.jsx` already ends
  that block with `return primaryDish ? (…) : null`, and the chat card already emits `''` for
  an empty list. **An absent row says *we don't know*; the boilerplate said something false.**
  The **review** half is deliberately untouched — *"★★★ Michelin Guide 2025 · curated
  recommendation"* describes where the row came from, which is true — and a test asserts it is
  still there, so scope creep fails the suite.

- **[THE PART THAT CHANGED THE SEVERITY — IT WAS NOT ONLY A RENDER PROBLEM]** `needsNarrate` is
  computed **above** the force-fill, so it correctly saw the real, short dish list. But
  `warmMichelinEnrich`'s cache **WRITE reads `v.dishes` at FIRE time**, and the force-fill
  mutated it before the fire. **On a narrate miss the fabricated names were written to Redis
  for 7 days and read back as real** on later taps — through `filterDishNames`, which as
  measured above lets them through. So the comment on that write, *"the cache stores the REAL
  narrated dishes (not the fast-path boilerplate)"*, was **true only when the narrate
  succeeded**. It is kept and amended rather than rewritten: the gap between what a comment
  claims and what the code does is the part worth seeing, and this is the second such comment
  in two entries (`user-prefs.js` was the first). Removing the boilerplate at source removes
  the poisoning as well — there is nothing fabricated left for the cache to keep.

- **[O-339, FOUND IN THE BLOCK IMMEDIATELY BELOW]** The review boilerplate hardcodes
  **`Guide 2025`**. Measured across the corpus: **2,099 venue-awards are 2026, 627 are 2025**,
  so the line is wrong for most venues it renders on. **v0.61.375 already fought this exact
  string once** — operator: *"drop the hard-coded 2025"* — and fixed the catalogue's copy; this
  one lives in another function and nothing went looking for a second. Filed, not fixed:
  O-338 was scoped to the dish row, and dropping-versus-deriving the year is a copy decision.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `__tests__/michelin-dish-forcefill.test.js` — 7 tests. Comment
  lines are stripped before every absence check, because both this file and `index.js` now
  *quote* the removed code; an absence check that reads commentary as code can never pass.
  It also runs the chat renderer on a dishless venue and asserts the card still carries its
  name and area with **no `🍲` and no bullet**. **Three mutations, each fired:** restoring the
  force-fill, tidying away the AU-1 record, and removing the review half too (the scope-creep
  guard). `npm test -- --run` → **197 files, 4,524 tests green**.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at **v0.62.826**, on the same branch as v0.62.825 (PR #1780, CI green on
  `357553d3` at four of four before this commit).

- **[TEST]** +7. 4,517 → 4,524.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **O-339** — the stale `Guide 2025`. **O-337** — the three `fr`/`en`
  ternaries that buy their strings; still the operator's spend call. **O-336** — 207 cuisine
  names missing in id/ru/de. Carried: O-333, O-334, O-332, O-226, O-331, O-328 by decision,
  O-325's half-attested closure, Tamil, the map pill, O-308, O-310 – O-313, O-184.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | The dish row only. The review row is left standing **and asserted to be standing**; O-339, found while in the same block, was filed rather than folded in. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | Deletion of generated text only; the removed code is preserved verbatim in place. No external call, no spend. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | AU-1 on the removed block and on the false comment; three mutations before landing. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | `filterDishNames` was **run** on the four strings rather than reasoned about, and it accepted all four — which is what settled removal over filtering. |

### [AMD-46] Tier 1 — the hours line follows the toggle, and the design I proposed did not survive contact

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"tier 1"*, from their question: *"If I switch the language
  to another in Cuisine TMA, do you think we can translate the display on the spot (I don't
  mean refresh (search) as refresh will bring another set of eateries)."*

- **[WHAT WAS ALREADY TRUE]** Most of the card already followed the toggle. `useLocale()` is
  React state, so every `t(key, lang)` string re-renders the instant it is tapped. What did
  **not** was anything the server had already rendered into a string — and of those nine
  fields, the hours line is the only one that is **pure computation over data the server
  already holds**. That is why Tier 1 was Tier 1.

- **[X — THE DESIGN I PUT TO THE OPERATOR FAILED AT THE BUNDLER, AND IS RECORDED AS PROPOSED]**
  The plan said: ship the **inputs** (`periods` + `utcOffsetMinutes`, which the server deletes
  at three sites), share `open-hours.js` into `web/_shared/lib/`, format at render — which
  would have fixed **staleness** as well as language. I argued it was near-risk-free because
  `open-hours.js` has zero `require`s and the TMAs already import from `_shared/lib/`.

  A throwaway probe settled it in one build:

  ```
  "ohLang" is not exported by "../../open-hours.js", imported by "src/__probe.js"
  ```

  The root package is CommonJS; `web/cuisine` is `"type": "module"`. Rollup will not take
  named imports from the CJS file. **Converting `open-hours.js` to ESM moves the break onto
  four CJS server call sites and its own test, and a second implementation is exactly the
  drift O-335 was about** — so neither was done.

  **What shipped instead:** the labels are rendered server-side once **per locale** and the
  client picks. `closedTodayByLang` / `currentOpenByLang`, keyed from `OH_LANGS` so this
  cannot become a fifth hand-copied list. **374 bytes a venue, measured on a real label set —
  less than the 561 the periods would have cost.** The freshness half is lost, and the code,
  the Register and this entry all say so rather than leaving it to be discovered.

  Worth naming plainly: **I recommended this to the operator as the low-risk tier and one of
  its two premises was wrong.** It was wrong in a way a five-minute probe exposed, which is
  the argument for probing before recommending, not after.

- **[DELTA]** `open-hours.js` — `_byLang` + the two exported builders. `cuisine-enrich.js`,
  the Michelin path and the free-text path each attach the map beside the scalar.
  `ResultCard.jsx` reads `venue.*ByLang?.[lang]` and **falls back to the scalar**, so a cached
  payload or an older deploy renders exactly as before.

  **The scalars are kept, not replaced.** Copy-All posts a whitelisted venue back to the
  server, which re-renders the chat message with `formatVenueBlock` in the **chat** language
  and reads the scalar. Replacing it would have degraded copy silently while the card looked
  right — the O-305 shape from the other direction. A test pins the two one-for-one.

- **[X — A GATE OF MINE PASSED WHILE HALF ITS SUBJECT WAS DELETED (O-341)]** The first draft
  asserted the three paths attach the maps with `toContain`. **Each file attaches each label
  twice** — the explicit `openNow` branch and the `openNow`-UNKNOWN branch added at v0.62.x —
  so `toContain` matched the second and **passed while the first was deleted**.

  It was caught only because the mutation run printed the occurrence count (`2 → 1`) and the
  deletion was **verified to have applied before the green was trusted**. A mutation that
  silently fails to mutate is indistinguishable from a gate that fires, and I had already
  written one such non-mutation earlier in this same session. Replaced with per-file counts
  plus the maps-track-scalars assertion. **Second time in two versions that the fix was
  settled by running the thing rather than reasoning about it** — O-338's `filterDishNames`
  was the first.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `__tests__/hours-locale-tier1.test.js` — 11 tests. It asserts
  the eight are **eight different strings**, not eight fallbacks to English (a falling-back
  table returns a string for every locale and passes any "is it a string" check); that the
  map is **empty rather than eight blanks** when there is nothing to say; that the builder
  **loops the derived list** with no locale named by hand below it; and it exercises the
  fallback chain rather than asserting it — map hit, unknown locale (`ko`), old payload,
  and `{}` with no throw. **Six mutations, each fired**, the sixth being the one that exposed
  the weak assertion above. `npm test -- --run` → **198 files, 4,535 tests green**;
  `web/cuisine` rebuilt.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at **v0.62.827**. Carries `[AMD-45]` forward per **D-205**.

- **[TEST]** +11. 4,524 → 4,535.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **Staleness is not fixed** — the labels still age from search time.
  **Tier 2** (bounded vocabularies) and **Tier 3** (a `relocalise` endpoint patching text
  without re-running the search, reusing the `onPatch` merge that already ships) are
  untouched; Tier 3 shares **O-337**'s spend question. **O-342** — `reopenStart`/`reopenEnd`
  are written on every closed venue and read by nothing, measured. **O-339**, **O-336** open.
  Carried: O-333, O-334, O-332, O-226, O-331, O-328 by decision, O-325's half-attested
  closure, Tamil, the map pill, O-308, O-310 – O-313, O-184.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Tier 1 only. Tier 2 and 3 untouched; O-342, found while enumerating the same fields, was filed rather than swept in. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | Additive response fields; no external call, no spend. The scalar every other consumer reads is kept and asserted. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | Six mutations, and the one that mattered was only trusted after its deletion was verified to have applied. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | The bundler failure was **produced by a probe**, not predicted; the 374-byte figure measured on a real label set, not estimated — my own estimate of ~320 was wrong. |

### [AMD-48] MRT line names — two of twelve, and the two are all there is

- **[INTENT]** Operator: *"medium effort, MRT lines only, hold the hawker centres"*.

- **[DELTA]** `web/_shared/lib/mrt-lines-i18n.generated.js` (2 rows, zh/ms/ta) +
  `scripts/fetch-mrt-line-names.mjs`, sibling of the station fetcher over the same endpoint —
  which had been correctly discarding these two rows as "not stations". Wired at **all seven**
  transport render sites: App ×2, LineStatusPanel, AffectedTicker ×2, EngineeringList,
  SystemMap. **Nothing translated by us.**

- **[THE SOURCE MISLABELS ONE ROW; THE COPY KEEPS IT]** The register writes
  **`North-South Line (EWL)`** — right name, wrong code. The join is on the **name**, bracket
  stripped. Reading the bracket files 南北线 under the East-West Line: a wrong answer that
  looks official. The mislabel is stored verbatim and **asserted as a premise**, so a source
  correction fails the test rather than leaving a comment to rot. The regenerator **throws**
  on an unmapped line instead of skipping it — a row the register starts publishing must not
  disappear into a filter.

- **[REACH, MEASURED]** The register publishes zh/ms/ta; the apps offer
  en/fr/de/ru/id/zh/ja/es. So **zh renders**, **`id` reads the `ms` column** (the mapping the
  station table already made, kept identical so the two cannot drift), and **`ta` reaches no
  reader** — stored anyway, because a copy of a register keeps what it copies. Two lines ×
  two reachable locales is the honest size of this.

- **[HAWKER CENTRES — HELD, AND THE REASON IS SOURCING, NOT EFFORT (O-344)]** Verified live:
  **43 categories, and the only place-name one is MRT/LRT Station.** No hawker, market or
  food-centre category exists. 123 centres × 3 languages has no free authoritative answer, and
  hand-curation is not translation — *Dhoby Ghaut* is 多美歌, *Bras Basah* is 百胜, neither
  derivable from the English. This repo has already watched a test written from memory guess
  *Bras Basah* wrong. 369 confident wrong strings is worse than none.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `__tests__/mrt-lines-i18n.test.js` — 17 tests. The pinned
  ten-missing list is checked **against `mrt-lines.js`**, so a thirteenth line cannot land in
  neither list; the mislabel is asserted as a premise; every locale the register does not
  publish is asserted to fall back to English, never blank; and no bare `{line.name}` may
  survive in a rendered position. **Four mutations, each fired** — trusting the bracketed
  code, dropping the `id`→`ms` mapping, reverting one render site, and making the regenerator
  skip instead of throw. **My own arithmetic was wrong first:** the wiring count asserted
  `n + 1` "for the import", and the import has no paren — it failed on all five files and the

  window. **Tamil absent by decision** — no register publishes it, no locale renders it, and

  Asserted, with a mutation that routes the key through the localiser and fails. A wrong
  string here costs a hint, not the ability to find the place — which is the design choice

### [AMD-50] O-344 second tranche — 39 → 111, and the twelve that stay English

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"do the remaining 84 hawker centres"*.

- **[METHOD CHANGED IN ONE RESPECT, AND IT MATTERED]** In the first tranche the station
  register was a **check applied afterwards**, and it caught two errors. This time it was an
  **input**: I pulled the register's characters for every locality first — 阿裕尼, 宏茂桥,
  勿洛北, 明地迷亚, 文礼, 牛车水, 金文泰, 友诺士, 芽笼峇鲁, 后港, 加基武吉, 高文, 马林百列,
  马林台, 红山, 淡滨尼, 丹戎巴葛, 直落布兰雅, 大巴窑, 合乐, 文庆, 美华 — and wrote from them.
  The cross-check now covers **40 rows**, up from 18. **Zero spelling errors in this tranche**,
  against two in the last.

- **[DELTA]** 72 rows added; **111 of 123** centres now carry a curated name. 41 `est`,
  70 `comp`.

- **[THE TWELVE ARE PINNED AT 111, NOT 123]** ABC Brickworks, Albert Centre, Empress Road,
  Jalan Batu, Kukoh 21, Mei Chin Road, Kebun Baru ×2, Anchorvale Village, Beo Crescent,
  Chomp Chomp, Kallang Estate. Names I could not stand behind. They render English only, and
  **the test asserts 111** — a count of 123 would mean they had been guessed.

- **[A RULE NARROWED ON EVIDENCE, NOT LOOSENED FOR CONVENIENCE]** The no-Latin-inside-CJK
  assertion flagged four **correct** rows: 沈氏通道79／79A座, 友诺士弯4A座, 马林台50A座,
  新樟宜上段路208B座. Singapore block numbers carry a letter suffix and are written that way
  in Chinese. A Latin letter is now allowed **only when it directly follows a digit** —
  `安谷village` and `Blk210` both still fail, proved by mutation. The distinction is real,
  not a weakening: the rule now says what it always meant.

- **[FOUR TEST DEFECTS, ALL IN THE TEST]** The tranche broke four of its own assertions and
  every one was the test's fault: a null-fallback case using a centre now curated; a matcher
  case asserting null for Chinatown Complex, which now correctly resolves **through the
  parenthetical** (a better assertion, so it was rewritten to expect 牛车水大厦巴刹); and two
  cross-check pairs that were simply wrong — **Yuhua is 裕华, not Chinese Garden's 裕华园**,
  and **Chong Pang** is a Yishun sub-estate whose name contains no 义顺. Worth noting the
  shape: a gate widened over new data tells you as much about the gate as the data.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** 50 tests; three further mutations each fired — a locality
  swapped for a different real place, an English word in a block address, a curated key
  matching no centre. `npm test -- --run` → **200 files, 4,602 green**; hawker rebuilt.

### [AMD-52] O-345 — the overlay withholds its words, and "settled" needed its own flag

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"take (b) for o-345"* — of the three routes filed, the
  small one that trades a wordless moment for a wrong language.

- **[DELTA]** `useLocaleHydrated()` in the Cuisine TMA's i18n, and the loading overlay gated
  on it. **Words withheld, not the card:** the shell, its height and the blinking dots stay,
  so nothing jumps when the sentence arrives and a blank screen is impossible. The 🛑 Stop
  button shows **the glyph alone** until then — usable, and not labelled in the locale that
  is about to be replaced.

- **[THE FLAG ONE LINE AWAY WAS THE WRONG ONE]** `serverHydrated` is set `true` **before**
  the await — deliberately, so a second caller does not re-fetch. Reusing it as "has it
  settled?" would report settled while the request is still in flight. So a separate latch,
  and it releases three ways:

  | Release | Covers |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | `finally` | offline, 401, 404 — the rejection paths |
  | 1500 ms ceiling | a request that **never resolves**, which `finally` cannot see |
  | module-level latch | repeat mounts settle synchronously — no blank frame after the first load |

  Gating UI on a promise is how you ship UI that never renders; two of those three exist
  only because of that.

- **[VERIFIED, NOT ASSUMED]** That this was **not** two storage keys: all five TMAs store
  under `LOCALE_KEY = 'gia.locale'` on one origin, asserted per app. The diagnosis in
  `[AMD-51]`'s reply was reasoned; this is the check.

- **[AND v0.62.825 IS WHY IT BECAME VISIBLE]** Before it, the route rejected `ja` with a
  400, so the server could never hold `ja` and there was nothing for hydration to correct
  **to**. The flash existed and could not be seen. My own fix made the mismatch real, and
  the code, the Register and this entry all say so rather than presenting O-345 as an
  unrelated find.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `__tests__/locale-hydration-gate.test.js` — 14 tests. It
  asserts the **premise** (`serverHydrated = true` occurs before the `await`, by position),
  the branch **order** (the gate arm must precede the rotating arm, or that text renders
  first), and that the card still renders. **Four mutations, each fired**: drop the
  `finally`, drop the ceiling, break `markLocaleSettled`'s idempotence, un-gate the Stop
  label. `npm test -- --run` → **201 files, 4,616 green**; cuisine rebuilt.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at **v0.62.831**. Carries `[AMD-51]` and the O-337 amendment forward.

- **[TEST]** +14. 4,602 → 4,616.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **The other four TMAs are untouched** — the overlay that was reported is
  Cuisine's, and only Cuisine has one that paints before any interaction. If another grows
  one, it needs the same hook. **O-337** now also carries the display-versus-key finding:
  `pipeline.js:1204` takes `venue.name` from Places' `displayName`, so changing
  `languageCode` changes the **key**, not just the label. O-344's 70 `comp` rows still want
  a native reader. Korean parked. O-342, O-339, O-336, O-334.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Route (b) exactly; (a) and (c) untouched, the other four TMAs left with a reason rather than swept in. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | Client-only render gate; no spend, no external call. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | Four mutations, and the two release paths exist because the failure mode was named first. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | The one-key premise was **asserted per app**, and the before-the-await ordering measured by index rather than by reading. |

### [AMD-55] The register reaches further than its station category — three rows I believed were wrong

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"use the Singapore government translation for 4 languages
  if in doubt"*, with the URL.

- **[X — MY "NO REGISTER FOR HAWKER CENTRES" WAS RIGHT ABOUT CENTRES AND WRONG ABOUT
  LOCALITIES]** I had checked the 43 **categories**, found only MRT/LRT Station, and concluded
  there was nothing else to use. A **free-text** search across all categories returns Community
  Clubs, libraries, constituencies, museums and heritage trails — which do carry locality
  names. My first free-text probes returned 0 for everything, *including `MRT`, which has 195
  rows*, because I sent JSON where the endpoint wants form-encoding. **A zero from a malformed
  request looks exactly like a zero from an empty index**, and I read it as the second.

- **[THREE CORRECTIONS, EACH AUTHORED AND BELIEVED]**

  | Locality | I wrote | The register says | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | Kebun Baru | 甘榜峇鲁 | **哥本峇鲁** | CC **and** SMC — two independent entries |
  | Pek Kio | 百吉 | **白桥** | Pek Kio Community Centre |
  | Taman Jurong | 裕廊坊 | **达曼裕廊** | CC **and** Our Museum@Taman Jurong |

  Plus **安谷** (Anchorvale CC) and **加冷** (Kallang CC) confirmed. Four rows leave the low
  tier — **41 est · 74 comp · 8 low** — and the pinned worklist **shrank from twelve to
  eight**, which is exactly what it was built to do.

- **[X — THE INSTRUCTION CAME WITH A TRAP, AND FOLLOWING IT NAIVELY WOULD HAVE ADDED ERRORS
  WHILE LOOKING LIKE VERIFICATION]** **Singapore school names are not locality evidence.**
  Alexandra Primary is **雅德**小学; Bukit Merah Secondary **达善**中学; Serangoon Garden
  Secondary **实勤**中学; Bendemeer Primary **明智**小学; Boon Lay Garden Primary **文园**小学;
  Dunman High **德明**政府中学. Six cases where the school's Chinese name has nothing to do
  with its locality's. Reading those rows would have "corrected" **Dunman → 德明** and
  **Bendemeer → 明智**, both wrong, each with an official citation attached — a worse outcome
  than the guesses they replaced, because it would have looked verified. Only Community
  Clubs/Centres, MRT stations, libraries and constituencies track the place name, and that
  rule is now a test.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** 56 tests. The three corrected spellings are pinned **as values,
  with the wrong one named**, so a later tidy-up cannot restore the guess. Two further
  mutations, each fired: restoring the authored spelling over the register's, and taking the
  school name for Dunman. `npm test -- --run` → **201 files, 4,622 green**; hawker rebuilt.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at **v0.62.833**, on the branch with #1784.

- **[TEST]** +4. 4,618 → 4,622.

  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | Free public reads, no spend. Four rows' characters changed — toward an official source. |

  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | The corrections came from **running the search correctly** after finding my own earlier probe was malformed — the zero I had trusted was my own request, not the index. |
