### [AMD-56] Codex found two real defects in the locale gate I shipped — and both were in the change that claimed to remove a jump and a flash

- **[INTENT]** No operator instruction. A **stale check-in fired for #1783, which had already
  merged**, and re-reading it surfaced a Codex review that had completed at **02:06:10Z** —
  after the merge, on a PR nobody had reason to open again. Two **P2** findings, both against
  `v0.62.831`, which is code I wrote in `[AMD-52]`. I verified both against source before
  touching anything, and both are correct.

  The uncomfortable part goes first, because burying it would be the failure this Journal
  exists to prevent: **the change these defects live in is the change that promised to remove
  a wrong-language flash and a layout jump.** It removed neither cleanly. My own fourteen
  assertions on that file passed throughout.

- **[DELTA]**

  **Finding 1 — `web/cuisine/src/v2/lib/i18n.js`. The ceiling started a clock; it did not stop
  the race.** Codex, verbatim: *"When `/api/cuisine/user-language` takes longer than 1.5
  seconds but eventually succeeds, this timer marks the locale ready and exposes the locally
  stored language while the request is still pending. The response is neither cancelled nor
  ignored, so [it] subsequently replace[s] that language and recreate[s] the exact
  wrong-language flash this gate is intended to prevent."*

  Exactly so. `setTimeout(markLocaleSettled, HYDRATE_CEILING_MS)` released the words at
  1,500 ms and then did nothing else; the late response still ran `dispatchEvent`. So the
  gate **did not remove the flash — it moved it**, from the first paint to a second and a half
  in, where a reader who has begun reading gets the sentence swapped underneath them. That is
  worse than the bug, not better. A guard whose failure mode is the thing it guards against.

  Fixed by making the ceiling record that it fired (`hydrateCeilingFired`) and splitting what
  a late answer is allowed to do: **stored to `localStorage`, never dispatched.** The session
  keeps the locale it opened in — which on this device the reader chose — and the *next*
  launch opens in the server's, from a value already on disk with no network wait at all.
  Choosing which of the two promises wins is the whole substance of the item; applying the
  answer late keeps a promise about correctness by breaking the one the gate was built to make.

  **Finding 2 — `web/cuisine/src/v2/App.jsx`. The placeholder reserved one line for a branch
  that emits two.** Codex, verbatim: *"During hydration this placeholder reserves only one
  text line, but the ready branch can contain two lines (for example, the first-device
  `ratingReminder`) and localized loading strings can wrap. Because the card is vertically
  centered, it therefore grows and moves when `localeReady` changes, contrary to the intended
  no-jump behavior."*

  Also correct, and directly contradicted by the comment I had written above it. `rotating`
  renders `loading.head` **and** the rotating line; the initial arm renders `loading.initial`
  **and** the rating reminder. Fixed with `min-h-[2.25rem]`, and stated at the width it
  actually holds: this removes the **one-versus-two-line** jump. A locale whose sentence wraps
  to a third line still moves the card, and no fixed min-height fixes that without clipping.
  Over-claiming here is precisely how the first comment came to be wrong.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** **The tests moved from grep to behaviour**, for the first time
  on this path — because the finding was about *timing*, and a grep can see
  `if (!hydrateCeilingFired)` sitting in the file while proving nothing about **when** it is
  consulted. There is no jsdom here (`vitest` runs `environment: 'node'`), so the four browser
  globals the module touches are stubbed by hand, the clock is faked, and
  `hydrateFromServerOnce` is exported as a test seam. Four scenarios: a response inside the
  window is applied; a response after it is **not** dispatched; it **is** stored; and a request
  that never resolves still releases the UI.

  **14 → 19 tests.** Four mutations, **each verified to have applied by occurrence count
  (1 → 0) before being run**, and each killed its intended assertion — dropping the guard,
  never setting the flag (killed two), making the store conditional, and removing the
  `min-h`. `npm test -- --run` → **201 files, 4,627 green**; cuisine rebuilt clean.

  **And the JSX comment was checked independently rather than trusted to the build's green.**
  I wrote a `//`-style comment inside the ternary arm; a `//` in JSX *children* position is
  **not a syntax error** — it renders as visible text on the page, so a passing build proves
  nothing. Minifying the transform output shows my marker **gone** while a deliberately
  mis-placed control marker **survives**: it is a real comment, in expression position. This
  session has twice been handed a green that meant something other than it appeared to; that
  is now the default assumption rather than a lesson.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at **v0.62.834**, on the branch with #1784.

- **[TEST]** +5. 4,622 → 4,627.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** The third-line wrap is unaddressed and is now stated rather than glossed.
  The eight `low` hawker rows and the native reader's pass stand. Korean parked. O-342, O-339,
  O-337, O-336, O-334.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Both findings verified against source before editing; both accepted rather than argued down. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | No spend, no external calls. One internal function exported as a test seam, noted in-file. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | Four mutations, each proved to have applied by occurrence count before being run. D-205: this record ships with the substantive change, not as its own PR. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | The JSX edit was verified by minified transform output, not by the build's exit code — the failure mode it guards against is silent, not loud. |

### [AMD-57] The fix for [AMD-56] was green locally and red in CI, and the reason was an artifact my sandbox had that CI never does

- **[INTENT]** No operator instruction. `3867c583` reported **201 files, 4,627 green** in the
  sandbox and CI's `Vitest unit tests` failed on the same SHA within a minute of the push.
  Same code, same command, opposite verdict.

- **[DELTA]** `Failed to load url react (resolved id: react) in web/cuisine/src/v2/lib/i18n.js`.
  **CI runs `npm ci` at the ROOT ONLY** — `web/cuisine/node_modules` does not exist on the
  runner. `i18n.js` imports `react` for its two hooks, so the moment `[AMD-56]`'s behavioural
  tests began *importing the real module* instead of reading it as text, the suite acquired a
  dependency the root install has never provided. My sandbox has that folder only because I
  have been building the TMAs all session.

  This is the same failure shape as `[AMD-56]`'s JSX check, arriving from the opposite side.
  There I distrusted a green and it turned out honest; here I trusted one and it was resting on
  a build artifact. **A green is a statement about the environment that produced it**, and the
  environment that matters is the one that has never run a TMA build.

  Fixed with a **file-scoped `vi.mock('react', …)`**, not a global `resolve.alias`. Two reasons.
  A global alias would hand a fake React to any future test that genuinely wanted a real one,
  silently — the class of trap this session keeps walking into. And the stub **throws if either
  hook is actually called**: none of the four scenarios renders one, so a test that starts to
  needs to fail loudly rather than pass on a lie. Adding `react` to root `devDependencies` was
  the alternative and was rejected: a production manifest should not grow a UI framework so that
  one test file can import a module it only needs four functions from.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** The CI condition was **reproduced locally** — `web/cuisine/node_modules`
  moved aside — and the identical error observed before anything was changed, rather than the fix
  being inferred from a log. Then the full suite run in **both** states: **201 files, 4,627 green
  with the folder absent, and 4,627 green with it restored.** The mutation removing the `vi.mock`
  (occurrence count 1 → 0, verified) reproduces the CI failure exactly.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at **v0.62.835**, on the branch with #1784.

- **[TEST]** ±0. The count is unchanged at 4,627 — this made an existing test honest in the
  environment that judges it, which is worth saying plainly rather than dressing as new coverage.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** Nothing now guards *against this class* — a root-suite test that imports a
  `web/**` module with its own dependencies will pass locally and fail in CI again, and it will
  look exactly like this. A CI-parity check would catch it; it is not built, and this entry is
  the record that it is owed rather than a claim that the risk is closed. Carried from
  `[AMD-56]`: the third-line wrap, the eight `low` hawker rows, the native reader's pass, Korean
  parked, O-342, O-339, O-337, O-336, O-334.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | The failure was reproduced before it was fixed; the fix is scoped to one file, not the config. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | No spend. `node_modules` was moved aside and restored, verified by re-running the suite after. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | Mutation proved to apply by occurrence count, and the suite run in both environments rather than the one that agrees with me. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | The CI log named the module and the path; the local reproduction produced the identical error string. |

### [AMD-59] The map and the chrome learn the reader's language — and [AMD-57]'s "nothing guards this class" was wrong

- **[INTENT]** Operator, from a Japanese session over Tokyo, with three screenshots,
  verbatim: *"i set to japanese, but the google map embedded is still english, wateriest
  are english. some components are still not translated"*. Scope chosen by the operator
  from a ranked plan: the small fixes **plus** `ArrivalPlate`, and the Maps language
  parameter set to **follow the app locale** — a visible-behaviour choice, so theirs.

- **[DELTA — THE CAUSE WAS NOT A MISSING TRANSLATION, AND THAT IS THE FINDING]** All five
  TMA tables were audited: **499 base keys, Japanese coverage complete.** The only two
  absences — `tile.sketchbook.label` ("Sketchbook") and `footer.brand` ("Soleat") — are
  already *pinned as deliberate* proper nouns. Every English word in those screenshots
  came from a string that **never went through `t()` at all**: **211 hardcoded
  `lang === 'fr' ? … : …` ternaries across 24 files**, written when the app had two
  locales and never revisited when it reached eight. For the other six languages every
  one renders the English arm.

  **The map.** Not one of the four Maps loaders passed `language` at all, so Google fell
  back to Accept-Language. New `web/_shared/lib/gmaps-language.js`, imported by all four —
  `gmaps-loader`, cuisine `MapPanel`, hawker and transport. Bare `zh` is **not** a Google
  code, so it maps to `zh-CN` (the app's Chinese is Simplified throughout); anything
  unknown becomes `en`, never `undefined`, because `language=undefined` and no parameter
  are different requests and the second is what we had. `public/app.js` is **excluded on
  purpose** — it is the legacy non-TMA surface with **zero** references to `gia.locale`,
  so a language parameter there would have nothing to read.

  **The card's cuisine word.** "Italian · ★4.7 · $$" under Japanese chrome — and the
  translation had existed for all 69 slugs the whole time. `ResultCard` renders Google's
  `primaryTypeDisplayName`, an English string with no slug on it, so `cuisineName()` could
  not be reached from it. New `restaurantTypeName()` slugifies the English word, which
  **is** the join (every catalogue slug is `slugify(name)`), plus 26 hand-listed venue
  types Google returns that are not cuisines — Cafe, Bar, Bakery, and "Indian", which the
  catalogue splits into north and south and so has no plain slug for.

  **The pill.** "1 gems" came from `InsightStrip`'s **inline** variant, which uses a short
  local `WORD` table — not the `tn('insights.gems')` path, which had `ja` all along. Two
  sources for one word; only one was extended when zh/ja/es shipped, and `|| WORD[k].en`
  turned the omission into plausible English rather than anything that failed.

  **`ArrivalPlate`.** 35 sites, **14 distinct strings**. `MEAL_BUCKETS`, `COUNTRY_LABEL`
  and `TIER_LABEL` extended to eight locales and routed through `localisedBody()` — the
  helper v0.62.781 added *for exactly this defect class* and applied at only four sites.
  The heading became a `{country}` template because English and French inflect it as an
  adjective and Japanese and Chinese do not inflect at all. `const fr` is now dead and
  removed. Plus `link.googleMap` for both infowindows, and the four `lastCard.*` keys.
  **19 new keys**, each in all seven blocks.

- **[CORRECTION — `[AMD-57]` SAID SOMETHING FALSE, QUOTED HERE VERBATIM PER AU-7]**

  > *"**Nothing now guards this class** — a root-suite test that imports a `web/**` module
  > with its own dependencies will pass locally and fail in CI again … A CI-parity check
  > would catch it; it is not built, and this entry is the record that it is owed."*

  **`__tests__/test-import-graph-guard.test.js` guards precisely this class and always
  did.** Its header quotes the identical error string. It scans every test file
  automatically; it missed mine because `IMPORT_RE` matches only **static** imports with a
  literal specifier, and v0.62.834 used `await import(I18N_PATH)` with a computed path. So
  the remedy was never "build a CI-parity check" — it was to widen a guard that already
  existed, which is far smaller and lands where a reader would look. That wrong sentence
  shipped in a Journal entry, a Register row and #1784's body; it is corrected rather than
  quietly dropped.

  The guard now follows dynamic imports — literal (`import('./api.js')`, a **real** edge:
  `i18n.js` reaches `api.js` that way and the guard was not following it either) and
  computed-via-`new URL(...)`. It also treats a `vi.mock('<spec>')` in the declaring test
  as satisfying the specifier, because that is the remedy CI proved works; forbidding it
  would push the next author toward a global `resolve.alias`, which silently hands a fake
  React to a test that wanted a real one.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** **The widened guard was run before the exemption was added
  and it caught v0.62.834's test immediately** — the defect CI found and it had missed.
  Mutation-proved both ways: removing the `vi.mock` makes the guard fail again.

  **19 new tests. Six mutations, each verified to have applied by occurrence count before
  being run** — and one, `M3`, **refused to apply** because my target string was wrong,
  which is the assertion catching itself rather than reporting a false pass. Two of my own
  assertions were wrong and were corrected against the source, not the other way round:
  a single-line check called `gmaps-loader` a failure when the parameter was correctly on
  the next line, and I asserted `COUNTRY_LABEL` had 16 rows from memory when it has 15.

  `npm test -- --run` → **202 files, 4,646 green** (from 4,627), run in **both**
  environments — with every `web/*/node_modules` moved aside, per `[AMD-57]`, and with
  them restored. All five TMAs rebuilt clean.

  **Three of my measurements were wrong before one was right**, and only the corrected one
  was reported: parsers claiming "450 keys missing `ja`", then "219", were artefacts of the
  files' shape — a base `{en, fr}` table plus separate per-locale blocks, with **mixed
  single/double quoting** inside `RU_STRINGS` and `DE_STRINGS` from an older automated
  pass. A regex assuming one quote style read the Russian block as 14 keys when it holds 233.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at **v0.62.836**.

- **[TEST]** +19. 4,627 → 4,646.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **The map cannot re-language mid-session.** The SDK is injected once and
  its `language` is fixed at that moment; Google offers no way to re-language a loaded map,
  and reloading it would blank and re-tile under the reader's finger. So switching locale
  leaves the map's tile labels in the language the session started in until the next launch.
  Asserted in the tests rather than left for a screenshot to find.

  **~176 fr-only ternaries remain** in the other 22 files (`App.jsx` 76, `ResultPanel` 21,
  `CuisineCategoryDrawer` 8, hawker/menu/clipboard/transport). Not in this scope by the
  operator's choice, and now measured rather than estimated.

  `restaurantTypeName` falls through to English for **id/ru/de** — that is **O-336**, pinned
  as an assertion so closing it fails *here* and must update that line deliberately. Venue
  names, dish names and romaji addresses are **O-337**, still awaiting the operator's ruling
  (it costs spend, and `venue.name` doubles as a lookup key). Dish names in the classics
  list are a `city-plates.js` data problem, untouched. Eight `low` hawker rows; the native
  reader's pass; Korean parked. O-342, O-339, O-334.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Ranked plan put to the operator; they chose "Small + ArrivalPlate" and "follow the app locale". Neither widened afterwards. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | No spend. `node_modules` moved aside and restored, verified by re-running the suite after. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | Six mutations, each proved to apply by occurrence count — one refused and was fixed rather than reported as passing. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | The i18n audit was re-done three times until it matched the shipped app; the guard was proved to fire on the real defect before the exemption was added. |
  | G5 — visible behaviour approved | ✅ | The Maps language parameter changes every user's map in every locale, so it was put to the operator as its own question rather than folded into the scope answer. |

### [AMD-61] All 168 hardcoded ternaries keyed — and the sweep's own first pass shipped a crash

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"do the remaining 176 ternaries"*, then — after I reported
  that 48 of `App.jsx`'s chains were already seven-locale and converting them was a refactor
  with real regression risk and no user-visible gain — *"do all 75"*. Both asked for; the
  second was asked for **after** being told the cost, which is why it was done rather than
  argued.

- **[DELTA — TWO CORRECTIONS FIRST, BECAUSE BOTH CHANGE WHAT THE WORK WAS]**

  **1. The sweep's first pass put a `ReferenceError` on `main`.** v0.62.836 removed
  `const fr = lang === 'fr'` from `ArrivalPlate` after converting its ternaries, but one
  **multi-line** site still read `{fr\n ? … : …}`. That identifier was then undeclared: the
  `plate.honestEmpty` branch would throw and blank the panel. **esbuild and Rollup both compile
  it happily** — it is only wrong at runtime. And the assertion written to prove the shape gone
  used `/fr \? ['"`]/`, which cannot see a condition split across lines. So a test, a build and a
  green CI all agreed on something false at once. Fixed, and guarded by a scan for bare
  identifiers nothing declares — the guard mutation-proved against the exact code that shipped.

  **2. The inventory was not what I had reported.** 168 sites in 22 files, not 176 — and
  `App.jsx`'s 75 are **not two-locale**. They were already chained (`lang === 'fr' ? … :
  lang === 'id' ? …`), and **53 of its 75 carried all seven** non-English locales. So *"for the
  other six languages every one renders the English arm"* — which shipped in #1785's body and
  Register O-346 — was true of the small files and **false of the largest one**. The genuinely
  broken surface was ~93 two-locale sites plus **22 chains with specific gaps: 20 missing `id`
  alone**, so Indonesian readers saw English there.

  **The work.** 93 sites across 21 files keyed by hand-authored keys; then all 75 in `App.jsx`.
  **162 new keys** total. `SEED_LABEL`, `MEAL_BUCKETS`, `COUNTRY_LABEL`, `TIER_LABEL` and
  `InsightStrip`'s `WORD` extended to eight locales. `menu`'s `i18n` gained a `tn` (its `t` had
  no interpolation); `clipboard`'s `t` already took vars, so it uses that rather than a second
  helper. `station-card-utils` got a **local** `EXIT_WORD` table because that module is
  deliberately React-free — and the `mrt.exitWord` key added minutes earlier was **removed**
  rather than left beside it, since two sources for one word is exactly the split that produced
  the "1 gems" bug.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** **`App.jsx` was not hand-edited.** A parser read all 75 chains
  into their locale arms — **75 parsed, 0 failed** — and 69 were converted mechanically, keys
  generated *from the arms themselves*, so no translation was retyped and none could drift.
  Six were done by hand: three carry a country name nested inside each locale arm (now
  `country.SG`/`MY`/`OTHERS` keys), and **four reorder their slots between locales** — zh and ja
  put the radius before the count — which a positional `{p1}/{p2}` scheme would have silently
  transposed. Those use named parameters.

  **The decisive check: every generated key was rendered in all eight locales and diffed against
  the arm it replaced. 552 comparisons, zero drift.** Then every one of the 69 call sites was
  checked to supply a variable for each placeholder it uses — an unsupplied one would print a
  literal `{p1}` on screen. 69 checked, 0 unsupplied. **Both of my first checking scripts were
  themselves wrong** and reported false alarms — one required the call on a single line, one
  matched `t(` and so missed every `tn(` — and each was corrected against the source rather than
  the source against it.

  `npm test -- --run` → **202 files, 4,651 green**, run in **both** environments (every
  `web/*/node_modules` moved aside, and restored). All five TMAs rebuilt clean.

  The existing suite caught two things I had wrong: the **form-of-address gate** flagged five
  German strings mixing *du*- and *Sie*-forms (now all formal, plus one the gate's
  case-sensitivity would have let through), and the **pinned key counts** failed and were updated
  deliberately rather than loosened.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at **v0.62.838**.

- **[TEST]** +5 across the arc. 4,646 → 4,651.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **Zero fr-conditional sites remain**, asserted as a whole-tree scan rather
  than a file list, so a *new* file reintroducing the pattern fails rather than being out of
  scope. The map still cannot re-language mid-session (the SDK is injected once). `id`/`ru`/`de`
  still fall through to English for cuisine *names* — **O-336**, pinned. Venue names, dish names
  and romaji addresses are **O-337**, awaiting the operator's ruling. The classics dish list is a
  `city-plates.js` data problem. Eight `low` hawker rows; the native reader's pass; Korean
  parked. O-342, O-339, O-334.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | The refactor half was costed to the operator before it was done, and done only once they said "do all 75". |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | No spend. `node_modules` moved aside and restored, verified by re-running the suite after. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | The runtime-identifier guard is mutation-proved against the exact code that shipped. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | 552 rendered strings diffed against the arms they replaced, not inferred from a passing build — the build was what missed the crash. |

### [AMD-62] The codebase audit — and the answer to the operator's original screenshot was an ABSENT string, not a hardcoded one

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"thorough check all the codebase for hard-coded foreign
  language that ought to be dynamic translated"*. Scope of the fix chosen by the operator from
  the findings: **the LLM localisation only (7 sites), all six locales at once** — explicitly
  **not** the 116 display-string ternaries also found.

- **[DELTA — THE HEADLINE FINDING IS NOT A HARDCODED STRING]** Every prompt that told the model
  which language to write in was shaped `lang === 'fr' ? <a French instruction> : ''`. French
  got an instruction, English needs none, and **the other six locales got an empty string** — so
  the model wrote English. **Seven sites**: `pipeline.js` ×2 (`vibe` / `dishes` /
  `signature_dish`), `gemini-client.js` ×3 (chat reply, hidden-gems narration, dish explainer),
  `vibe-summary.js` ×2 (the sanctuary summary **and its cache key**).

  **This is why the operator's screenshot looked like a missing translation and was not one.**
  The card read `おすすめ: lobster cream pasta • crab arrabiata • tiramisu` — the label came from
  the i18n table and was correctly Japanese; the dishes came from the model and were English.
  Chasing it as a missing key would have found nothing, because the key was there and right.
  It is also **not O-337**: that is Google Places *data*, which costs money to re-request and
  carries the display-vs-key hazard; this is our own narration, and localising it costs nothing.

  **The cache key was half the bug, and the half that is easy to miss.** `vibe-summary.js`
  clamped its Redis dimension to `fr`/`en`, so a Japanese reader was served the **English cached
  summary**. Localising the prompt alone would have changed nothing for them — it would never
  have run. Both halves moved together; existing `:en`/`:fr` entries stay valid.

  New `prompt-locale.js` holds the rule once for seven callers, built on `langName()` from
  `translate-review.js`, which already maps all eight locales (and 27 more). **French's
  hand-tuned hidden-gems block is kept verbatim** and a generic sibling added for the other six:
  that block names exact label wordings (`"Address" → "Adresse"`) and French typographic rules,
  and folding it into the generic version to tidy the code would have **regressed a locale that
  works in order to fix six that did not**.

- **[WHAT THE AUDIT CLEARED, BECAUSE "NO FINDING" IS A FINDING]** A linear scanner read all 420
  tracked sources; **99 contain non-English literals and almost all are correct by design**:
  translation tables and `*-i18n*.generated.js`; the Michelin rosters and `instruction/*.js`
  (JP alone holds 1,180 CJK strings — native restaurant names, proper nouns); **native-language
  search aliases** (`榴莲店`, `пельмени`, `午市套餐`, `sầu riêng`) which are queries sent to
  Google, not display, and would break search if translated; language names in pickers
  (`Français`, `日本語`) which are correct in their own language; the per-locale maps built
  earlier in this arc; and LLM prompt *content*. Two were false positives — regex characters in
  `.vibe-journal/templates/app.js` and `generate.mjs`.

  **A stated limit of the method:** this scan finds *foreign text in source*. It cannot find
  **English** strings that should be keyed, because those read as ordinary code. Those were found
  by the separate `fr`-conditional inventory. Neither technique alone is sufficient, and saying
  so matters more than the tidy number.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** 11 new tests, **four mutations each verified to have applied by
  occurrence count** and each killing its intended assertion: reverting the gate, dropping the
  iconic-dish protection, re-clamping the cache key, and reverting one `langBlock`.

  **My own helper had a bug, caught by exercising it rather than reading it.** The first gate was
  `Boolean(langName(lang))` — and `langName('kr')` returns the uppercase fallback `'KR'`, since
  `kr` is not an ISO code and Korean is `ko`. That would have told the model to *"write in KR"*.
  Now gated on the app's own locale list, and pinned so it cannot widen back to `langName`'s 35.

  `npm test -- --run` → **203 files, 4,662 green**.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at **v0.62.839**.

- **[TEST]** +11. 4,651 → 4,662.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **116 display-string ternaries remain outside `web/`** — `index.js` 91, plus
  25 across ten modules — measured and left by the operator's scope choice. `index.js` already
  calls `t()`/`tn()` **209 times**, so the infrastructure is there and these bypass it. Seven of
  those 116 were the LLM sites fixed here, leaving **109**.

  Unverifiable from here, and stated rather than assumed: **whether the model actually obeys the
  new instruction well in each of the six languages**. The mechanism is proven — it is the same
  shape French has been running on — but the output quality per language is a thing only a reader
  of that language can judge. Same standing recommendation as the hawker names.

  Unchanged: the map cannot re-language mid-session; **O-336**; **O-337**; the `city-plates.js`
  dish-name data; eight `low` hawker rows; Korean parked. O-342, O-339, O-334.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Findings put to the operator first; they chose the 7 LLM sites and declined the 116. Neither widened. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | No spend. The change alters prompt TEXT, not model or call volume. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | Four mutations, each proved to apply by occurrence count before being run. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | The helper was executed across all eight locales and the junk cases, which is how the `KR` bug surfaced — reading it would not have. |

- **[INTENT]** No operator instruction. #1786 merged at **15:25:27 SGT** while `[AMD-62]`'s work
  was in progress. Recorded per the standing rule and **folded into that change per D-205**.

- **[DELTA]** Squash **`0ee16b78`**, `2026-08-30T15:25:27+08:00`, carrying **v0.62.837–838**:
  `[AMD-61]`, the completed 168-site ternary sweep, together with the `ReferenceError` fix and
  `[AMD-60]` — the #1785 merge record — folded forward. Third consecutive PR to carry one.

  `App.jsx` holds **zero** `lang === 'fr' ?` sites; the diff between this branch's base and
  `origin/main` is **empty**.

- **[AND THE PUSH WAS BLOCKED, CORRECTLY]** `git push` failed on a repo pre-push hook:

  > *"the remote branch tip is CONTENT-IDENTICAL to main, so its pull request has been merged.
  > A fast-forward push would append to a closed PR: it would succeed, and the work would never
  > reach main."*

  Exactly right, and worth recording because **the failure mode it prevents is silent**. The push
  would have reported success; v0.62.839 would have sat on a branch whose PR was closed, and
  nothing would have said so until someone wondered why the fix was not live. This is the same
  shape as the other near-misses this arc — a green that means something other than it appears —
  except this time the guard was already built and fired. The branch was restarted from
  `origin/main` and the commit replayed with `git format-patch` + `git am`, which applied
  cleanly, then re-tested.

- **[STATUS]** #1786 is **PROD** on `main` as `0ee16b78`; Railway deploys from `main`. This record
  is **DRAFT**, shipping with `[AMD-62]` rather than as its own PR.

- **[TEST]** ±0. 4,662 stands.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** Unchanged by the merge. The 109 display-string ternaries outside `web/`; the
  map's mid-session re-language seam; O-336; O-337; the `city-plates.js` dish data; eight `low`
  hawker rows; Korean parked. O-342, O-339, O-334.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

### [AMD-65] How to SAY it — a pronunciation line, which is not the translation line

- **[INTENT]** Operator, across three messages, and the third defined the feature:

  1. *"the restaurant name's second line should have the japanese way to pronounce the foreign
     resturant name"*
  2. *"use minimum token Gemini model call per venue per locale, including train line name,
     hawker centre, icon for translation"* (with an image attached)
  3. *"remember to help foreigner to pronoun. same for English or french speaker who searching
     Malaysia eateries, learn to pronounce the restaurant name"*

  I had put a spend question to the operator and they dismissed it, then answered it directly in
  message 2 — approval **with a cap**, which is a narrower grant than the question offered and is
  treated as such.

- **[DELTA — PRONUNCIATION IS NOT TRANSLATION, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE DESIGN]**
  `translate-name.js` already answers *"what does this name mean, and how would a reader in the
  venue's own country read it"*. Message 3 asks a different question — *"how do I say this"* — and
  the difference changes the gate. That module skips a Latin name as **"already readable"**, and
  message 3 is exactly the case that exposes: **"Restoran Sri Nirwana Maju" is Latin script and an
  English speaker still cannot say it.** So the new path is symmetric and deliberately **not**
  script-gated. A Japanese reader gets katakana for "White Bird Restaurant"; an English reader gets
  a phonetic guide for a Malay name.

  New `pronounce-name.js`, plus a `namePronounce` line on the cuisine card and in the bot template.

  **The operator's icon.** They sent a picture — a globe whose right half is cut into a speech
  bubble with three dots, arrows arcing down each side — not a character. There is no emoji for
  that: 🌐 is a globe with no speech, 💬 speech with no globe, and 🔤 is already the **translation**
  line's marker, so reusing it would make two different features look like one. So the Mini Apps
  get `PronounceIcon.jsx`, an SVG drawn to the image. **The bot uses 🗣**, because Telegram HTML
  cannot render SVG — an asymmetry recorded rather than hidden, since the alternative was degrading
  the apps to what the weakest surface can manage.

- **[THE COST CAP IS PART OF THE SPEC, SO IT IS TESTED LIKE ONE]** *"minimum token"* is honoured
  three ways, and each has an assertion — a cap that lives only in a comment is a cap nobody holds:

  **(a) Curated first, always.** The government register already gives Chinese and Malay names for
  all **123 hawker centres, 193 MRT/LRT stations and the MRT lines** — free, official, better than
  a model. A `zh` or `id` reader must never cost a call for those. Measured: `hawkerNameLocal`
  returns the name for zh/ms/id and **null for ja/es/de/ru/fr**, so only those five reach the model
  at all.
  **(b) Lite-first chain.** `gemini-models.js` orders its chain for *reliability*; this module
  reorders it for *price*, so `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` is tried first. Both orderings are deliberate
  and the test asserts they differ.
  **(c) One call per (name, locale), cached 30 days — including the NONEs.** A name needing no
  guide ("Pizza Hut" for an English reader) caches the sentinel. Caching only successes would
  re-ask every already-sayable name on every search, forever — the expensive mistake. Keyed on the
  **name**, not the placeId, so two branches called the same thing share one answer.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** 18 new tests with a call-counting Gemini stub, so *"did this cost
  anything"* is an assertion rather than a claim. **Four mutations, each verified to have applied
  by occurrence count**, each killing its intended assertion: dropping the lite reorder, ignoring
  the curated answer, caching successes only, and re-adding a script gate — the last failing four
  tests at once, which is the symmetry requirement doing its job.

  `npm test -- --run` → **204 files, 4,680 green**. All five TMAs rebuilt clean.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at **v0.62.840**.

- **[TEST]** +18. 4,662 → 4,680.

- **[KNOWN GAPS — AND ONE IS A PART OF THE ASK NOT YET DELIVERED]** The operator named **train line
  names and hawker centres**. For **zh and id those already work today**, client-side and free,
  from the curated tables. For **ja/es/de/ru/fr they do not**, and cannot yet: the hawker and
  transport Mini Apps read their names from bundled tables with **no server round-trip**, so there
  is no path from them to Gemini. Wiring that needs a new endpoint, which is real work and is
  **owed, not done**. Said plainly rather than left to look finished.

  Also unverifiable from here: **whether the guides are actually good** — whether the katakana
  scans naturally to a Japanese speaker, and whether the phonetic English is usable. The mechanism
  is tested; the output quality needs a speaker of each language.

  Unchanged: the 109 display-string ternaries outside `web/`; the map's mid-session re-language
  seam; O-336; O-337; eight `low` hawker rows; Korean parked. O-342, O-339, O-334.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Three messages reconciled; the third redefined the feature and the design follows it, not the first reading. |
  | G2 — no destructive action | ✅ | No deletions. `translate-name.js` is untouched and its 🔤 line still ships alongside. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | Four mutations, each proved to apply by occurrence count before being run. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | The cost guarantees are asserted with a call-counting stub, not asserted in prose. |
  | G5 — spend authorised | ✅ | Operator approved it **with a cap** ("minimum token … per venue per locale"), and the cap is implemented and tested, not just honoured in spirit. |

### [AMD-66] The hawker and train-line endpoint — closing the gap [AMD-65] left open and named

- **[INTENT]** Operator: *"do the hawker centre and train line endpoint"*. This is the part of
  O-348 I shipped as **owed rather than done**, and being asked for it is the record working —
  the gap was written down where the operator could read it, and they read it.

- **[DELTA]** `POST /api/pronounce`, plus `pronounce-client.js`, `use-pronounce.js`, and the two
  render sites.

  **AUTHENTICATED, deliberately.** It follows `verifyInitData` exactly as the other TMA POST
  routes do, rather than joining `/maps-key` on the unauthenticated side. **An open endpoint that
  spends Gemini per call is a bill anyone could run up**, and the standing instruction not to add
  a public route without asking points the same way. Batch capped at 60 names, each ≤120 chars,
  de-duplicated, locale validated against `APP_LOCALES`.

  **THE COST CONTROL LIVES ON THE CLIENT, AND THAT IS THE DESIGN.** The government register covers
  Chinese and Malay for all 123 hawker centres, 193 stations and the MRT lines — and **those
  tables are bundled in the apps**. So the client resolves its own curated answer and asks only
  for what it lacks: **a `zh` or `id` reader issues no request at all**, and a `ja` one sends only
  the names the register never covered. The saving is made before the network, let alone before
  the model. Doing the lookup server-side would also have meant requiring **ESM** `web/_shared`
  modules from a **CommonJS** `index.js` — the same constraint that killed the client-side
  open-hours plan earlier in this arc.

  **Three cache layers** — in-flight (two components mounting at once make one request), memory,
  and `localStorage` — and **a null is cached at every one**. "Needs no guide" is a real answer;
  caching only successes re-asks every already-sayable name forever. `cachedPronunciation()`
  returns `undefined` / `null` / string as **three distinct states**, because collapsing the first
  two is exactly how that mistake gets made.

  **The React hook is a SEPARATE file** from the client. `pronounce-client.js` imports nothing and
  is therefore exercised for real in the root vitest run; `use-pronounce.js` imports React and is
  not. That split is the lesson of v0.62.834 applied in advance rather than after CI found it.

  Both render sites show the say-it line **only where the register has nothing** — with a curated
  Chinese name present, that line already is the answer and a second would be noise.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** 16 new tests driving the real client with a call-counting fetch
  stub, so *"does a Chinese reader cost anything"* is an assertion rather than a claim. **Four
  mutations, each verified to have applied by occurrence count**, each killing its intended
  assertion: ignoring the curated answer (2 tests), not persisting the nulls, dropping the batch
  cap, and removing the endpoint's auth check.

  **The existing suite caught two things, and one was a fault in a guard I wrote today.**
  `map-and-chrome-i18n`'s bare-`fr` scan flagged `web/hawker/src/App.jsx` — a **false positive**:
  the strip removed `//` lines but not JSX `{/* … */}` blocks, and my new comment contains the
  locale list *"(ja/es/de/ru/fr)"*. Fixed the guard rather than reworded the comment: **a guard
  that cries wolf on prose gets its output ignored, which is how the next real one is missed.**
  And `mrt-lines-i18n` pins how many times each file calls `lineName`; mine went 1 → 4 (one in
  `curatedFor`, two in the guard, none a new render), updated deliberately rather than loosened to
  a `>=`, since the point of that table is that a new render site cannot appear unnoticed.

  **And one defect caught by staging the diff, not by any test.** `pronounce-client.js` contained three
  **literal NUL bytes** — my sentinel and key separator were written as raw `0x00` rather than escapes.
  Valid JavaScript, every test passing, and **git classified the file as BINARY**, so the entire module
  showed as an unreviewable blob in `git diff --numstat`. It would have been merged unread. Replaced with
  `\u0000` escapes: a sentinel must be a value no guide can equal, but it need not be unprintable in the
  source. Noticed only because the numstat line read `-  -` instead of a count.

  `npm test -- --run` → **205 files, 4,696 green**, in **both** environments. All five TMAs built.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at **v0.62.841**.

- **[TEST]** +16. 4,680 → 4,696.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **MRT stations are not wired** — only the *lines* were asked for and only the
  lines were done. The 193 stations have the same shape and the same free zh/ms coverage, so it is
  the same wiring again; named here rather than left to be discovered.

  Unchanged and still true: **whether the guides read well in each language** is not something any
  test here settles. The 109 display-string ternaries outside `web/`; O-336; O-337; eight `low`
  hawker rows; Korean parked. O-342, O-339, O-334.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Exactly the gap O-348 recorded as owed: hawker centres and train lines, not stations. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | No deletions. The new route is authenticated and bounded. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | Four mutations, each proved to apply by occurrence count before being run. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | The cost claim is asserted with a call-counting stub; the auth check is asserted by reading the route body. |
  | G5 — spend bounded as authorised | ✅ | The operator's cap is enforced at the cheapest point — the client does not send what the register already answers. |

### [AMD-68] #1789's CI caught a white-screen I shipped — and the checklist that should have caught it never knew the harness existed

- **[INTENT]** No operator instruction. This is the post-PR routine for #1789 finding a **real
  defect**, not a flake. Recorded because the interesting part is not the one-line fix; it is that
  this repo already built a harness for exactly this bug, after exactly this app white-screened in
  production, and I did not run it.

- **[THE DEFECT]** `web/hawker/src/App.jsx`. `[AMD-66]`'s new hook block was placed at the top of
  the component, above the `const active` on line 485 that it reads:

  ```js
  const centreNames = React.useMemo(
    () => (active?.centres || []).map(...),   // `active` is declared 180 lines BELOW
    [active?.centres]
  );
  ```

  `active?.centres` reads as defensive, and that is the trap: **optional chaining guards `null`
  and `undefined`, not the temporal dead zone.** Every Hawker launch threw
  `ReferenceError: Cannot access 'active' before initialization` and `#root` never painted. Not a
  degraded line — a blank app, for every user, in every locale.

- **[WHY EVERY CHECK I RAN WAS GREEN]** `node --check` reads syntax, and the syntax is valid.
  `vite build` compiles it — Rollup minified it to `pe` and emitted a clean 392 kB bundle.
  4,696 unit tests passed, including the 16 I wrote for this feature, because
  `pronounce-endpoint.test.js` asserts the *wiring* by grepping the source for
  `usePronunciations(centreNames, lang` — a string that was present, correct, and in the wrong
  place. **Every check I ran reads source or output. None of them ran the component.**

- **[THE PART WORTH KEEPING]** `scripts/render-smoke.mjs` does run it, and its own header says why
  it exists: v0.62.692, `renderCentreCard` referencing an undeclared `isShort`, which
  *"white-screened Hawker in production after passing `node --check`, `vite build`, 3,901 unit
  tests, every CI job"*. Register **O-120** created it in response. Same app, same class, same
  blank screen — and I did not run it, because **`gia-preflight` never mentioned it.** The
  checklist's step 0 says "if you touched `web/` → build", and a build is precisely the check that
  cannot see this. The harness was built and then left out of the list that decides what gets run.
  Fixed here, with both regressions quoted in place so the entry explains itself.

- **[DELTA]**
  - `web/hawker/src/App.jsx` — block moved below `const active`, with a comment recording *why*
    that position is load-bearing rather than incidental, so it is not "tidied" back up later.
  - `.claude/skills/gia-preflight/SKILL.md` — `npm run test:render` added to step 0, non-negotiable
    for any `web/` change, carrying both regressions as its justification.
  - `package.json` → **0.62.842**.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Mutation-proved, not assumed:
  1. Fix applied → `npm run test:render` → **all 6 apps painted** (hawker 76 nodes).
  2. Fix reverted with `git checkout` → rebuilt → **hawker failed**, identical error and identical
     bundle hash (`index-mWQguKtN.js`) to the CI run. So the harness detects *this* defect, and
     the local reproduction is the same artifact CI tested, not a lookalike.
  3. Fix restored → rebuilt → green again.
  - `npm test -- --run` → **205 files, 4,696 passed**. All 7 builds clean.

- **[STATUS]** **DRAFT** on `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf`, pushed to #1789.

- **[TEST]** ±0 unit tests. The check that mattered here was never a unit test, which is the point.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** Unchanged, plus one new: `pronounce-endpoint.test.js`'s wiring assertions are
  **source greps**, so they confirm a call exists and cannot confirm it runs. That is acceptable
  only because `test:render` now backs them; it is worth remembering that the two are not
  interchangeable. Still open: MRT stations unwired; whether the guides read well per language;
  the 109 display-string ternaries outside `web/`; O-336; O-337; eight `low` hawker rows; Korean
  parked. O-342, O-339, O-334.

### [AMD-69] The MRT stations — and the render harness earning its place twice in one hour

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: ***"do the mrt stations"***. The last of the three surfaces
  named in *"use minimum token Gemini model call per venue per locale, including train line name,
  hawker centre"*. Venues shipped in v0.62.840, hawker centres and lines in v0.62.841/842, and the
  193 stations were carried as an open gap in both PR bodies until asked for. **The gap was
  written where the operator could read it, and it was read — twice now.**

- **[DELTA]**
  - `web/transport/src/App.jsx` — `stationSayNames` + `usePronunciations`, batched for the whole
    focused line, passed down as a `say` string to both `StationCard` sites.
  - `web/transport/src/components/StationCard.jsx` — renders the guide under the name strip,
    behind `PronounceIcon`, **only when `displayName === name`** (the register had nothing).
  - `pronounce-name.js` — the prompt said `Restaurant name:` for every caller. A station is not a
    restaurant, and that is a false premise the model has to work around. Now `Place name:`.
  - `__tests__/pronounce-stations.test.js` — 13 new.
  - `package.json` → **0.62.843**.

- **[THE COST DECISION, WHICH IS WHY THIS IS NOT "HAWKER AGAIN"]** Stations differ from the other
  two surfaces in the one dimension the operator capped. The hawker list renders one panel and the
  line panel one name, so a hook inside the component is fine. **A focused line renders 20–35
  `StationCard`s at once.** The client keys its in-flight map on the *batch*, so a hook inside the
  card would make each card its own single-name request — **35 round trips for one line**, same
  model spend, 35× the network. So the fetch is hoisted to `App`, batched once per line, and the
  card takes a plain string. A test asserts the contrast directly: 35 names in one call, and the
  same 35 asked card-by-card producing 35 calls — *because an assertion that only shows the good
  number cannot tell you the bad one was ever possible.*

  `curatedFor` hands back the government register's Chinese/Malay name, so **a zh or id reader
  still costs nothing**. A test now reads the shipped table and asserts **all 193 rows** have both
  columns, rather than repeating the claim from the header comment.

- **[THE HARNESS EARNED ITS PLACE, AN HOUR AFTER I ADDED IT]** `[AMD-68]` added
  `npm run test:render` to `gia-preflight` because v0.62.842 shipped a temporal-dead-zone
  white-screen. Running it on this change caught a **second** crash, of the *opposite* kind:

  > **React #310 — "rendered more hooks than during the previous render". Transport, blank.**

  I placed the hook after `const lineStations`, correctly avoiding the TDZ that bit Hawker — but
  `lineStations` is computed **below** `if (!data) return <LoadingSkeleton />`. So the hook was
  skipped on the loading render and ran once data arrived. **4,709 unit tests and seven clean
  builds were green.** Only the harness failed.

  So the position is pinned **from both sides**, and the two constraints pull against each other:
  *below* its dependencies (or TDZ), *above* the early returns (or Rules of Hooks). The window is
  real but narrow, and neither boundary is visible to `node --check`, to Rollup, or to any unit
  test. The block now derives the line's stations itself instead of reading `lineStations`, since
  reaching for that value is exactly what dragged it below the returns.

- **[AND MY OWN TEST WAS ASSERTING THE WRONG INVARIANT]** The first version pinned only
  *"declared after every const it reads"* — which the broken code **satisfied**. It passed while
  Transport was blank. Rewritten to check both boundaries, then mutation-proved in both
  directions: hook pushed below the early returns → fails; hook lifted above `coarseStations` →
  fails. **A one-sided guard on a two-sided constraint is a guard that reports Passed on the
  failure it exists to catch.**

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Seven mutations, each verified to have *applied* (occurrence counts
  printed 1→0) before its effect was read, and each killing exactly one assertion: hook back inside
  `StationCard`; hook above `lineStations`; prompt reverted to `Restaurant name`; batch
  de-duplication dropped; the `displayName === name` render guard dropped; hook below the early
  returns; hook above `coarseStations`. **206 files, 4,709 passed.** Seven builds clean.
  `test:render` **6/6**.

- **[STATUS]** **DRAFT** on `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf`, shipping into #1789.

- **[TEST]** +13 (4,696 → 4,709).

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **O-348 is now closed** — venues, hawker centres, lines and stations all carry
  a pronunciation line. Still open: whether the guides actually *read well* per language (no test
  here settles it, and a native reader's pass is still owed, eight `low` hawker rows first); the
  109 display-string ternaries outside `web/`; O-336; O-337; O-342; O-339; O-334; Korean parked.
  New and minor: `doc/.serial-state.yml`'s rotation appends a zero instead of incrementing, so the
  `last_anchor_source_prior000…` chain has grown the file to 6,094 lines — raised with the
  operator, untouched pending a ruling.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | "do the mrt stations" — the named gap, wired the same way, with the batching difference the card count forces. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | No Cloud Translation re-run; no new public route (the existing authenticated endpoint is generic and unchanged). Cache key deliberately NOT bumped, so no paid-for guide is discarded. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | Journal + serial-state before the push; `gia-preflight` run in full, `test:render` included. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | Seven mutations, each proved applied then proved fatal; the React #310 crash reproduced and then shown fixed in the built bundle. |

### [AMD-71] The production log — a silent, total travel-times outage, and a log line that could not say why

- **[INTENT]** Operator attached a Railway log (`logs.1788079970683.json`, 245 lines,
  **30-08 '26 07:56:46 → 08:52:18 UTC**) with: ***"attach is the log"***. No question attached,
  so the task is to read it and report what is actually in it.

- **[WHAT THE LOG SAYS — measured, not summarised from memory]** 245 entries across two Railway
  services (app `f9a39516`, Redis `678921e6`) and three app deployments. 200 `info`, 45 `error`.

  | Finding | Count | Reading |
  | :--- | ---: | :--- |
  | `[travel-times] TRANSIT/DRIVE matrix failed: … 403` | **14** | **Total outage.** 14 calls, 14 failures, **zero successes**, in *both* deployments. |
  | `[Weather] v2 … returned empty; trying v1 fallback` | 15 | Degraded but working — all five datasets, v1 recovers silently. |
  | `[setMyDescription] … exceeded Telegram's 512-char cap` | 4 | Self-reported: en (487) and fr (496) are shipping **with the trailing hint line dropped**. |
  | `npm error … signal SIGTERM` | 5 | **Not a crash.** Old container `b320cdfa` stopping while `42790f2c` reported healthy — a normal rolling redeploy. |

- **[AND THE DEPLOYMENT NEWS, WHICH THE LOG ALSO CARRIES]** `09aa3c0b` booted **v0.62.842** at
  08:49:17 and reported `🟢 Healthy` / `🚀 soleat is live` at 08:49:22 — so `[AMD-68]`'s hawker
  fix and the pronounce endpoint are **in production and healthy**. Searches at 08:51 returned
  6–10 venues each. Nothing in the new deployment's log mentions pronounce, say or station.

- **[THE DEFECT WORTH FIXING, AND IT IS NOT THE 403]** All fourteen lines read:

  > `[travel-times] TRANSIT matrix failed: Request failed with status code 403`

  That is axios's generic `err.message`, and **it is byte-identical for every possible cause** —
  Routes API not enabled, key API-restrictions excluding it, billing lapsed, referrer/IP
  restriction. Google puts the real reason in the response *body*, and the handler read
  `err.message` and **threw the body away**. The one call that could tell these apart discarded
  the evidence that tells them apart.

- **[WHY I DID NOT NAME A CAUSE — the repo already learned this]** **Register X-9**
  (`register-0_62_722`), verbatim: *"Two hypotheses about the embedded map were stated before
  being measured, and both were wrong… The actual cause (API restrictions excluding Maps
  JavaScript API) came from three probes, not from reasoning."* Same key, same 403 class, and the
  recorded lesson is *"Diagnosis asserted ahead of measurement."* I have no key here and cannot
  probe, so naming a cause now would repeat X-9 exactly. **The fix is to make the next occurrence
  self-diagnosing**, not to guess ahead of it.

- **[DELTA]**
  - `travel-times.js` — `describeRoutesError(err)`, exported. Renders
    `HTTP 403 — PERMISSION_DENIED — <Google's sentence> — reason=API_KEY_SERVICE_BLOCKED`
    instead of `Request failed with status code 403`. Every read is optional, so an HTML proxy
    page or a bodyless network error degrades to the old string rather than to `undefined`.
  - `__tests__/travel-times-diagnostics.test.js` — 7 new. `travel-times.js` had **no tests at
    all** before this.
  - `package.json` → **0.62.844**.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Two mutations, each verified to have applied (occurrence counts
  printed) and each killing exactly one assertion: reverting to `err.message`; dropping the
  Google sentence and keeping only the status. One test asserts that the **four** distinct 403
  causes — which previously rendered byte-identically — now render as four different strings.
  Demonstrated end to end against a real Routes error shape:

  ```
  BEFORE (prod, 14x): Request failed with status code 403
  AFTER:              HTTP 403 — PERMISSION_DENIED — Requests to this API
                      routes.googleapis.com … are blocked. — reason=API_KEY_SERVICE_BLOCKED
  ```

  **207 files, 4,716 passed** (+7).

- **[STATUS]** **DRAFT** on `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf`, shipping into #1790.

- **[KNOWN GAPS / OWED TO THE OPERATOR]**
  - **The 403's root cause is an operator action in the GCP console, not a code change** — and it
    cannot be narrowed further until a request runs with this build. Deliberately not guessed
    (X-9). P7: no external account change made or attempted.
  - **The `setMyDescription` over-cap is real and unfixed.** The log's own text says *"Shorten the
    copy in index.js to control what goes"* — currently the trailing hint line is dropped for the
    operator rather than chosen by them. Left alone because it is **operator-facing copy**, and
    rewriting their words unasked is not mine to do.
  - **Weather v2 returns empty for all five datasets** and v1 covers it. Working, but the v2 path
    is effectively dead and the fallback is carrying production.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | A log with no question: read it, report it, fix what is mine to fix. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | No GCP/console/key change attempted. No operator copy rewritten. |

  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | Counts derived by parsing the attached JSON, not read off impressions; two mutations proved applied then fatal. |

### [AMD-72] The translations were never reaching the server — it was answering for the wrong reader

- **[INTENT]** Operator, three screenshots (ru / de / ja) on v0.62.842, verbatim:
  ***"i still dont see the translations . i change to japanese, french, russian"***.

- **[THE TELL, WHICH WAS IN THE SCREENSHOTS]** Every one of the three showed a **fully
  translated chrome** — «Кухня», „Küche", 料理, the filter chips, the open-hours line, the
  distance — and an **untranslated card**: no pronunciation line, no reading line, an English
  address. The boundary between translated and not fell **exactly on the client/server line**.
  That is not what a broken translation table looks like; it is what a *correctly working*
  server answering for a *different user* looks like.

- **[ROOT CAUSE]** `deviceLang` is `navigator.language` — **the PHONE's language**. The
  operator's phone is English; what they changed was the in-app locale toggle (`gia.locale`).
  So three name-enrichers on `/api/cuisine/search` (and one on the Michelin path) were told the
  reader was English:

  ```js
  await require('./pronounce-name').attachPronunciations(payload?.venues, deviceLang, { redis });
  ```

  The server then computed a pronunciation guide for an **English** reader of "The Canteen by
  Enjoy", correctly concluded none was needed, **cached the NONE for 30 days**, and rendered
  nothing. **Nothing failed. Nothing threw. Nothing logged.** The wrong question was answered
  perfectly, and the cache made the wrong answer durable — which is why no amount of reading
  `pronounce-name.js` would ever have found it.

- **[AND THE RIGHT VALUE WAS ALREADY THERE]** `csLang` — body `lang` from the toggle, else the
  stored per-user preference, else `'en'` — is computed **~2,500 lines earlier in the same
  function**, and v0.62.825 had already fixed it to cover all eight locales. `enrichCtx` uses it.
  `enrichSanctuaryRead` uses it. The handler computed the right value and handed these four calls
  the wrong one. Verified by parser (acorn) that `csBodyLang` is in the same function and
  declared before the new use — this session has shipped two ordering crashes already.

- **[THE TEST THAT SHOULD HAVE CAUGHT IT ASSERTED THE BUG]** `pronounce-name.test.js` carried:

  > `it('the search path attaches pronunciations in the READER's language', …)`
  > `expect(src).toContain("attachPronunciations(payload?.venues, deviceLang")`

  The title names the requirement. The expectation pins the value that **violates** it. It went
  green for five versions and reported Passed on the single thing that was broken. **A test that
  restates the implementation cannot fail with it** — the same lesson `[AMD-69]` recorded from
  the other direction, arriving here as false assurance rather than a missing check.

- **[DELTA]**
  - `index.js` — `const readerLang = csBodyLang || deviceLang || csLang;` used by
    `attachNameReadings`, `attachPronunciations` and `attachNameGloss`; the Michelin path's
    `attachNameReadings` now takes `csLang || michDeviceLang`. `deviceLang` is **kept as the
    middle fallback**, not deleted: with no body `lang`, the phone is still a better guess than
    `csLang`'s final `'en'`.
  - `__tests__/reader-language.test.js` — 9 new.
  - `__tests__/pronounce-name.test.js` — the false assertion corrected, with the reason written
    where the next reader will hit it.
  - `package.json` → **0.62.845**.

- **[SCOPE — measured, so this is a complete fix and not a partial one]** `deviceLang` had
  exactly **four** consumers in `index.js`, all four reader-facing name enrichers, all four now
  corrected. Everything else on these paths (`enrichCtx`, dish narration, open-hours labels) was
  already on `csLang`. So the diagnosis accounts for every symptom rather than the loudest one.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Four mutations, each verified to have applied and each killing its
  assertions: pronunciations back to `deviceLang` (3 failures); Michelin back to `michDeviceLang`
  (1); `csBodyLang` dropped from `readerLang` (2); the hand-copied 5-locale list restored (1).
  **208 files, 4,725 passed** (+16 across this arc).

- **[STATUS]** **DRAFT** on `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf`, shipping into #1790.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** The **30-day cached NONEs are already written** under the English key — a
  reader who searched before this fix gets the right answer immediately (a different key), but
  nothing purges the wrong-reader entries; they simply expire. Addresses stay English (**O-337**,
  Places `languageCode`, still awaiting a ruling). Station names in the map info card have no
  ja/ru/de because the register covers zh/ms only — expected, not a defect. **The Michelin path
  has no `attachPronunciations` at all**; adding it means Gemini spend on a new path, so it is
  named here rather than added unasked.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Three screenshots showing chrome translated and cards not; traced to the variable, not the feature. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | No cache purge, no key change, no new paid path. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | Journal + serial-state before the push. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | Scope measured by grep (4 consumers, 4 fixed); scope-safety proved with acorn; four mutations proved applied then fatal. |

### [AMD-73] The second line now follows the toggle — a different bug from [AMD-72], same symptom

- **[INTENT]** Operator: ***"the second line translation as I change the language and the map
  doesnt change on the fly when i change language"***. Two faults. This entry is the first; the
  map is a UX trade-off put to the operator rather than decided unilaterally.

- **[WHY THIS IS NOT [AMD-72] AGAIN]** `[AMD-72]` fixed **which** language the server is told.
  It did not make the answer **reactive**, and the two produce the same screenshot. The guide is
  computed server-side and baked into the search payload; changing the locale **does not re-run
  the search** (verified: `lang` appears in exactly two `useCallback` deps in the cuisine App and
  in no search effect). So the chrome re-rendered in the new language while the card's second
  line kept the answer for whichever locale the search happened to run under.

- **[DELTA]**
  - `web/cuisine/src/v2/App.jsx` — one batched `usePronunciations(venueSayNames, lang, …)` for
    the whole page, through the same `/api/pronounce` the Hawker and Transport apps use. `lang`
    is in the call, so a toggle re-resolves with no new search.
  - `web/cuisine/src/v2/components/ResultCard.jsx` — reads the answer synchronously out of the
    module cache and prefers it over the payload.
  - `__tests__/pronounce-reactive.test.js` — 9 new.
  - `package.json` → **0.62.846**.

- **[THE THREE-STATE READ, WHICH IS THE PART THAT COULD SILENTLY REGRESS]**

  ```js
  const said = cachedPronunciation(venue.name, lang);
  const sayNow = said !== undefined ? said : venue.namePronounce;
  ```

  Not `said || venue.namePronounce`. With `||`, a locale that legitimately needs **no** guide
  falls through and shows the guide for the locale the search ran under — `[AMD-72]`'s bug
  re-expressed one layer up. A mutation asserts exactly that.

- **[COST]** Near zero, and worth stating because the operator capped this. The server already
  wrote these to Redis under the same `(name, locale)` key during the search, so the client call
  resolves from **cache, not the model**; `localStorage` means any given pair is asked once,
  ever; and it is **one batched call per page** — a hook inside `ResultCard` would have been one
  request per card, the same trap v0.62.843 avoided for stations.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Three mutations, each proved applied then fatal: truthiness
  fallback; card rendering the payload field again; `lang` replaced by `'en'` in the hook.
  **209 files, 4,734 passed** (+9). Seven builds clean, `test:render` 6/6.

- **[TWO OF MY OWN MISTAKES, BOTH CAUGHT BY RUNNING THINGS]**
  1. The `initData` import used `'../../api/tg.js'`, copied from `lib/api.js` which sits one
     level deeper. **Rollup caught it**; `node --check` and the unit tests could not.
  2. `test:render` then passed on the **stale** bundle, because the build had failed and the old
     one was still on disk — a green that meant nothing. Re-run after a successful build. *A
     render check is only as current as the last successful build*, and that is not obvious from
     its output.
  3. My ordering assertion searched the whole file for `\n  return (` and matched a **helper
     defined above the component**, so it failed while the code was correct. Anchored to App's
     own return. Wrong test, not wrong hook.

- **[STATUS]** **DRAFT** on `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf`, shipping into #1790.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **The map still does not change language on the fly, and cannot be made to
  without a decision.** Google Maps JS API fixes `language` at script-injection time
  (`gmaps-loader.js` guards on a module-level `pending` and on `window.google.maps`); the API has
  no supported way to change it afterwards, and re-injecting is explicitly unsupported. The only
  mechanism is a page reload, which **loses the current results** — only `gia.lastSgAnchor` is
  persisted, not the venue list. Put to the operator rather than chosen for them.

### [AMD-74] #1790 merged, and Codex found two real bugs in it — one of them the operator's own complaint, one layer down

- **[INTENT]** No operator instruction. #1790 was marked ready and **auto-merged 40 seconds
  later**; Codex's review completed ~2 minutes after the merge. Both findings verified against
  the source, both real, both mine. This entry carries the merge record (per D-205, folded into
  the fix rather than shipped as a docs-only PR — seventh consecutive) and the follow-up.

- **[MERGE RECORD]** Squash **`2fd9454f`**, carrying **v0.62.846**: the 193 MRT stations
  (`[AMD-69]`), the travel-times 403 diagnostic (`[AMD-71]`), the reader-language fix
  (`[AMD-72]`), the reactive second line (`[AMD-73]`), and `[AMD-70]` folded forward.
  **Verified by content, per D-123:** empty diff between `b4c4302b` and `origin/main`;
  `package.json` at **0.62.846**; `readerLang` ×4 in `index.js`; `sayNow` ×3 in `ResultCard.jsx`;
  `describeRoutesError` ×4 in `travel-times.js`; `stationSay` ×4 in the transport App.

- **[CODEX P1 — and this one stings]** `usePronunciations` kept its own **accumulating** Map: a
  `useState` initializer (which runs **once**, so it never re-seeded for a new locale) plus an
  effect that **merged** each fetch into the previous map and **returned early when a fetch came
  back empty**. So switching from a locale with guides (ja) to one that needs none (en) left
  every Japanese guide on screen under the English UI, indefinitely.

  **That is the operator's own complaint — "it doesn't change when I change language" — living
  one layer below the fix that was supposed to close it.** v0.62.846 made the *cache*
  locale-correct and left the *consumer* accumulating. I verified the client cache and the
  cuisine card and never checked the hook's own map, so Hawker and Transport kept the bug the
  same day I reported it fixed.

- **[THE FIX IS STRUCTURAL, NOT A PATCH]** The map is now a pure **projection** of the
  `(name, locale)`-keyed cache, so it *cannot* carry another locale's answer — there is no
  accumulator left to go stale. The projection lives in `pronounce-client.js` (which imports
  nothing) rather than in the hook, **so it is unit-tested for real** instead of grepped: the
  root suite cannot import React, and a source-grep is exactly the weak check `[AMD-72]` was
  written about.

- **[CODEX P2]** The cuisine memo keyed on `placeId`. When a Michelin entry's Places lookup
  fails, `index.js` emits the venue with `placeId: ''` and a real name — so two consecutive
  pages of N such venues produced the **same separator-only dependency string** while every name
  changed, pinning the memo to the previous page. Premise verified in `index.js` (and pinned by
  a test, so the fix does not outlive its own justification). Now keyed on the names.
  **The rule that avoids the class: derive the dependency from the same values the memo
  returns** — then the key *is* the content and the two cannot disagree.

- **[DELTA]** `web/_shared/lib/pronounce-client.js` (+`projectPronunciations`),
  `web/_shared/lib/use-pronounce.js` (projection, unconditional version bump),
  `web/cuisine/src/v2/App.jsx` (`venueSayKey`), `__tests__/pronounce-locale-switch.test.js`
  (8 new), `__tests__/pronounce-reactive.test.js` (one assertion re-pointed at the requirement
  rather than the moved expression). `package.json` → **0.62.847**.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Three mutations, each proved applied then fatal — a projection
  that falls back to any locale's answer (3 failures); nulls no longer persisted (1); the
  `placeId` dependency restored (1). The first reproduces Codex's P1 exactly and the tests fail
  on it, so they test the reported behaviour and not merely the new code.
  **210 files, 4,742 passed.** Seven builds clean, `test:render` 6/6.

- **[STATUS]** **DRAFT**, branch restarted from `origin/main` after the merge.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** The map's language still needs a page reload; a **plan was put to the
  operator and is awaiting approval** — snapshot `pages`/`cursor` to `sessionStorage`, reload,
  restore through the existing `useEffect([cursor])` replay path, with a "never reload if it

### [AMD-76] A pipe in a venue name — the fourth route to the same symptom, found by Codex

- **[INTENT]** Codex P1 on #1791, raised **after** that PR had merged. Operator, in the same
  window: *"what needs to be done now? what are the outstanding works"* — and this was the
  answer, so it was done rather than listed.

- **[THE BUG]** The pronunciation path round-tripped the name list through a pipe:
  `names.join('|')` in `use-pronounce.js`, then `dep.split('|')` in both consumers. **A venue
  whose name contains a pipe was torn into two unrelated names.** The cache filled under the
  fragments while `ResultCard` looked up the whole name, so the guide never appeared — and the
  stale search-payload line survived a locale change.

- **[WHICH IS THE SAME SYMPTOM, BY A FOURTH ROUTE]** v0.62.845 fixed *which language* the server
  was told; v0.62.846 made the answer *reactive*; v0.62.847 stopped the map *accumulating*; and a
  pipe in a name would **still** have shown the operator a stale second line. Four independent
  causes, one indistinguishable symptom — the same shape as the original complaint, which turned
  out to be three unrelated faults wearing one face.

- **[THE PREMISE WAS RE-READ, NOT TRUSTED]** Codex named
  `Ji De Chi 记得吃甜品 | Square 2 Novena`; that string was confirmed in
  `data/durian-variance/durian-pastry-gemini-verified-2026-05-30_1143.json` before any change,
  and a test now pins it so the fix cannot outlive its justification.

- **[SCOPE IS WIDER THAN THE REVIEW SAID]** Codex flagged the cuisine App. But the
  `join`/`split` lives in **`use-pronounce.js` since v0.62.841**, so **Hawker and Transport were
  affected too** — any hawker centre or line name containing a pipe. Fixed at the root rather
  than at the reported site.

- **[AND THE FIX IS NOT A RARER DELIMITER]** Any character can occur in a name, so a "safer"
  separator only relocates the failure somewhere less testable. **The list is no longer rebuilt
  from a string at all.** `JSON.stringify` serves only as a dependency key, where escaping makes
  collision impossible; the array itself is passed through untouched. A test states the point
  directly: `['a|b'].join('|') === ['a','b'].join('|')`, and the JSON forms differ.

- **[DELTA]** `web/_shared/lib/use-pronounce.js` (`stableList`, JSON dep),
  `web/cuisine/src/v2/App.jsx` (`venueNamesRaw` + JSON key),
  `__tests__/pronounce-pipe-names.test.js` (7 new), and two earlier tests re-pointed at the
  requirement. `package.json` → **0.62.848**.

- **[MY OWN TESTS KEEP PINNING EXPRESSIONS — THIRD TIME]** Two existing tests failed on this
  refactor while the behaviour they name was intact, because they pinned the exact key
  expression rather than *"the batch key is built from venue names"*. Re-pointed at the
  requirement. And the new file's negative assertions first failed on **this module's own
  comment** describing the bug being fixed — the same shape as the bare-`fr` guard that once
  flagged a comment listing locale codes. **Fixed the check, not the prose**: comments are
  stripped before the negative match, because a guard that cries wolf on documentation is a
  guard whose next real hit gets ignored.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Three mutations, each proved applied then fatal: the hook back to
  `join`/`split` (1 failure); the client splitting names on a pipe before sending (3); the
  cuisine key back to a pipe (1). **211 files, 4,749 passed.** Seven builds clean,
  `test:render` 6/6.

- **[STATUS]** **DRAFT**, carrying `[AMD-75]` forward per D-205.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** Unchanged, and the map-language reload plan **remains unapproved and
  unstarted**. Worth noting for the operator: **both #1790 and #1791 merged while their Codex
  review was still running**, and both reviews then returned real P1s. On this repo Codex is a
  post-merge audit, not a gate.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**
